\displaystyle\hat{H}^{N,3}_{i}(\xi^{N}_{0},u_{N},\Phi_{s})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\emptyset\neq A\subset{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}r^{N,s}(\sqrt{N}A)\widehat{E}\Big(h_{i,3}^{N,+}(\xi^{N}_{0},u_{N},A,\Phi_{s})-h_{i,3}^{N,-}(\xi^{N}_{0},u_{N},A,\Phi_{s})\Big),

we obtain the decomposition

\hat{H}^{N,j}(\xi^{N}_{0},u_{N},\Phi_{s})=\sum_{i=2}^{|\bar{\mathcal{N}}|}\hat{H}^{N,j}_{i}(\xi^{N}_{0},u_{N},\Phi_{s}).

(7.30)

We will now obtain simple bounds on the summands in
(
7.30
), leaving a more detailed analysis of the main
terms
\hat{H}^{N,2}_{2}(\xi^{N}_{0},u_{N},\Phi_{s})
and
\hat{H}^{N,3}_{3}(\xi^{N}_{0},u_{N},\Phi_{s})
to
Section
7.3
.

Lemma 7.4
.

There are constants

C_{\ref{dN2i}}
,
C_{\ref{dN3i}}
such that for any
T>0
,

\phi\in C_{b}([0,T]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2})
and
s\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\Large|\hat{H}^{N,2}_{i}(\xi^{N}_{0},u_{N},\Phi_{s})\Large|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\ref{dN2i}}\|r\|\|\Phi_{s}\|_{\infty}(\log N)^{1-\binom{i}{2}}X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1}),\quad 2\leq i\leq|\bar{\mathcal{N}}|,

(7.31)

\displaystyle\Large|\hat{H}^{N,3}_{i}(\xi^{N}_{0},u_{N},\Phi_{s})\Large|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\ref{dN3i}}\|r\|\|\Phi_{s}\|_{\infty}(\log N)^{3-\binom{i}{2}}X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1}),\quad 3\leq i\leq|\bar{\mathcal{N}}|,

(7.32)

and

\displaystyle|\hat{H}^{N,3}_{2}(\xi^{N}_{0},u_{N},\Phi_{s})|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\ref{dN3i}}\|r\|\|\Phi_{s}\|_{\text{{Lip}}}(\log N)^{(6-p)/2}X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1}).

(7.33)

Proof.

We start with the observation that for
\emptyset\neq A\subset{\mathcal{N}}_{N}
and
any
a\in A
,

|I^{N,\pm}_{i}(x,u_{N},A,\xi_{0}^{N})|\leq\xi^{N}_{0}(B^{N,x+a}_{u_{N}})1\{|B_{u_{N}}^{N,x+\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}|=i\}+\xi^{N}_{0}(B^{N,x}_{u_{N}})1\{|B_{u_{N}}^{N,x+\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}|=i\}.

(7.34)

We will bound
E\big(|h^{N,\pm}_{i,j}|\big)
with this
inequality. By translation invariance,

\displaystyle\frac{\ell^{(j)}_{N}}{N^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}|\Phi(s,x)|\widehat{E}\big(\xi^{N}_{0}(B^{N,x+a}_{u_{N}})1\{|B_{u_{N}}^{N,x+\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}|=i\}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{\ell^{(j)}_{N}}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}\sum_{w\in{S_{N}}}|\Phi(s,x)|\xi^{N}_{0}(x+w)\widehat{P}\big(B^{N,x+a}_{u_{N}}=x+w,|B_{u_{N}}^{N,x+\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}|=i\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{\ell^{(j)}_{N}}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}\sum_{w\in{S_{N}}}|\Phi(s,x)|\xi^{N}_{0}(x+w)\widehat{P}\big(B^{N,a}_{u_{N}}=w,|B_{u_{N}}^{N,\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}|=i\big)

\displaystyle\leq\|\Phi_{s}\|_{\infty}X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})\ell^{(j)}_{N}\widehat{P}\big(|B_{u_{N}}^{N,\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}|=i\big).

The same bound holds if we replace
B^{N,x+a}_{u_{N}}
with

B^{N,x}_{u_{N}}
, and thus we have shown that

\widehat{E}\big(|h^{N,\pm}_{ij}(\xi^{N}_{0},u_{N},A,\Phi_{s})|\big)\leq 2\|\Phi_{s}\|_{\infty}X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})\ell^{(j)}_{N}\widehat{P}\big(|B_{u_{N}}^{N,\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}|=i\big).

(7.35)

By Remark
1.7
, the lower bound on
u_{N}
in
(
7.12
), and the fact that
w_{N}\geq 1/2
for
N
large,

\ell^{(j)}_{N}\widehat{P}\big(|B_{u_{N}}^{N,\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}|=i\big)=\begin{cases}O((\log N)^{1-\binom{i}{2}}),&j=2,\\
O((\log N)^{3-\binom{i}{2}}),&j=3.\end{cases}

Using this bound in (
7.35
) above, and substituting into
(
7.29
) we obtain (
7.31
) and (
7.32
).

The proof of (
7.33
) is more delicate, as it relies on
cancellation. For
x\in S_{N}
define

\Omega^{N}_{x}(A)=\{\sigma^{N}_{x}(A,\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}\setminus A)>u_{N},\tau^{N}_{x}(A,\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}\setminus A)<u_{N}\}.

(7.36)

If
|B^{N,\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}_{u_{N}}|=2
, and
B^{N,x+A}_{u_{N}}
and

B^{N,x+\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}\setminus A}_{u_{N}}
are disjoint,
then

B^{N,x+\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}\setminus A}_{u_{N}}=B^{N,x}_{u_{N}}
and
for any
a\in A
,
B^{N,x+A}_{u_{N}}=B^{N,x+a}_{u_{N}}
.
Thus

\displaystyle I^{N,+}_{2}(x,u_{N},A,\xi_{0}^{N})-

\displaystyle I^{N,-}_{2}(x,u_{N},A,\xi_{0}^{N})

\displaystyle=\Big(\xi^{N}_{0}(B^{N,x+a}_{u_{N}})(1-\xi^{N}_{0}(B^{N,x}_{u_{N}}))-(1-\xi^{N}_{0}(B^{N,x+a}_{u_{N}}))\xi^{N}_{0}(B^{N,x}_{u_{N}})\Big)1\{\Omega^{N}_{x}(A)\}

\displaystyle=\Big(\xi^{N}_{0}(B^{N,x+a}_{u_{N}})-\xi^{N}_{0}(B^{N,x}_{u_{N}}\Big)1\{\Omega^{N}_{x}(A)\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{w\in{S_{N}}}\xi^{N}_{0}(w)1\{B^{N,x+a}_{u_{N}}=w\}1\{\Omega^{N}_{x}(A)\}-\sum_{w\in{S_{N}}}\xi^{N}_{0}(w)1\{B^{N,x}_{u_{N}}=w\}1\{\Omega^{N}_{x}(A)\}.

Now by translation invariance,

\widehat{E}[I^{N,+}_{2}(x,u_{N},A,\xi_{0}^{N})-I^{N,-}_{2}(x,u_{N},A,\xi_{0}^{N})]\\
\\
=\sum_{w\in{S_{N}}}\xi^{N}_{0}(w)\widehat{E}[1\{B^{N,a}_{u_{N}}=w-x\}1\{\Omega^{N}_{0}(A)\}]-\sum_{w\in{S_{N}}}\xi^{N}_{0}(w)\widehat{E}[1\{B^{N,0}_{u_{N}}=w-x\}1\{\Omega^{N}_{0}(A)\}].

Plugging into the definition of

h^{N,\pm}_{2,3}(\xi^{N}_{0},u_{N},A,\Phi_{s})
gives

\displaystyle\widehat{E}\big((h^{N,+}_{2,3}-h^{N,-}_{2,3})(\xi^{N}_{0},u_{N},A,\Phi_{s})\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{(\log N)^{3}}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{w\in{S_{N}}}\xi^{N}_{0}(w)\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}\Phi(s,x)\Big[\widehat{E}\big(1\{B^{N,a}_{u_{N}}=w-x\}1\{\Omega^{N}_{0}(A)\}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\widehat{E}\big(1\{B^{N,0}_{u_{N}}=w-x\}1\{\Omega^{N}_{0}(A)\}\big)\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{(\log N)^{3}}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{w\in{S_{N}}}\xi^{N}_{0}(w)\widehat{E}\Big[(\Phi(s,w-B^{N,a}_{u_{N}})-\Phi(s,w-B^{N,0}_{u_{N}}))1\{\Omega^{N}_{0}(A)\}\Big].

By the above,

\displaystyle\Big|\widehat{E}\big((h^{N,+}_{2,3}-h^{N,-}_{2,3})(\xi^{N}_{0},u_{N},A,\Phi_{s})\big)]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(\log N)^{3}}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{w\in{S_{N}}}\xi^{N}_{0}(w)\widehat{E}\big(\big|\Phi(s,w-B^{N,a}_{u_{N}})-\Phi(s,w-B^{N,0}_{u_{N}})\big|\big)

\displaystyle\leq\|\Phi_{s}\|_{\text{{Lip}}}\frac{(\log N)^{3}}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{w}\xi^{N}_{0}(w)\widehat{E}\big(|B^{N,a}_{u_{N}}-B^{N,0}_{u_{N}}|\big)

\displaystyle=\|\Phi_{s}\|_{\text{{Lip}}}X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})(\log N)^{3}\widehat{E}\big(|a|+|B^{N,0}_{2u_{N}}|\big)

\displaystyle\leq\|\Phi_{s}\|_{\text{{Lip}}}X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})(\log N)^{3}\big(c/\sqrt{N}+\sqrt{\sigma^{2}4u_{N}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C({\mathcal{N}},\sigma^{2})\|\Phi_{s}\|_{\text{{Lip}}}X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})(\log N)^{(6-p)/2},

(7.37)

where
c=\max_{e\in{\mathcal{N}}}|e|
and we recall from (
3.13
) that

\widehat{E}\big(|B^{N,0}_{s}|^{2}\big)=2w_{N}\sigma^{2}s\leq 2\sigma^{2}s
. Using this bound in
(
7.29
) we obtain (
7.33
).
∎

In order to use the above results to effectively handle the
drift terms
d^{N,2}(x,\xi^{N}_{s},\Phi)
and

d^{N,3}(x,\xi^{N}_{s},\Phi)
we must first obtain bounds on the
first and second moments of the total mass, which will play
an important roll in what follows. Therefore we interrupt
our current analysis to handle the total mass next.

7.2
Total mass bounds

We now introduce a particular choice of
u_{N}
, namely

t_{N}=(\log N)^{-19}.

(7.38)

Lemma 7.5
.

There is a constant

C_{\ref{dN3cnd1}}>0
so that for
j=2,3
, all
T>0
and

\Phi\in C_{b}([0,T]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2})
,

\displaystyle|d^{N,j}(x,\xi^{N}_{s},\Phi)|\leq\|r\|\|\Phi\|_{\infty}|\bar{\mathcal{N}}|\ell_{N}^{(j)}X^{N}_{s}(\mbox{\bf 1})\quad\forall s\in[0,T]

(7.39)

and

\Big|E[d^{N,j}(s,\xi^{N}_{s},\Phi)|{\mathcal{F}}^{N}_{s-t_{N}}]\Big|\leq C_{\ref{dN3cnd1}}\|\Phi\|X^{N}_{s-t_{N}}(\mbox{\bf 1})\quad\forall s\in[t_{N},T].

(7.40)

Proof.

(
7.39
) holds by (
7.4
), while
(
7.40
) follows from Lemma
7.3
,
(
7.30
), and Lemma
7.4
.
∎

Proposition 7.6
.

There exists a

c_{\ref{mobnds}}>0
, and for
T>0
a constant

C_{\ref{mobnds}}>0
depending on
T
, such that for any
t\leq T
,

\displaystyle(a)

\displaystyle E[X^{N}_{t}(\mbox{\bf 1})]\leq(1+C_{\ref{mobnds}}(\log N)^{-16})X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})\exp(c_{\ref{mobnds}}t),

(7.41)

\displaystyle(b)

\displaystyle E[(X^{N}_{t}(\mbox{\bf 1}))^{2}]\leq C_{\ref{mobnds}}(X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})+(X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1}))^{2}).

Therefore, for
T>0
there is a constant
C_{\ref{stbnd}}>0
,
depending on
T
, such that for all
s,t\in[0,T]
,

E[X^{N}_{s}(\mbox{\bf 1})X^{N}_{t}(\mbox{\bf 1})]\leq C_{\ref{stbnd}}(X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})+(X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1}))^{2}).

(7.42)

Proof of (a).

By (
6.10
),

E[X^{N}_{t}(\mbox{\bf 1})]=X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})+\int_{0}^{t\wedge t_{N}}\sum_{j=2}^{3}E[d^{N,j}(s,\xi^{N}_{s},\mbox{\bf 1})]ds+\int_{t\wedge t_{N}}^{t}\sum_{j=2}^{3}E\Big[E[d^{N,j}(s,\xi^{N}_{s},\mbox{\bf 1})|{\mathcal{F}}^{N}_{s-t_{N}}]\Big]ds.

(7.43)

Use the two bounds from Lemma
7.5
(with
\Phi=1
)
and
\ell^{(j)}_{N}\leq(\log N)^{3}
in (
7.43
) to get

E[X^{N}_{t}(\mbox{\bf 1})]\leq X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})+2\|r\||\bar{\mathcal{N}}|(\log N)^{3}\int_{0}^{t\wedge t_{N}}E[X^{N}_{s}(\mbox{\bf 1})]ds+C_{\ref{dN3cnd1}}\int_{0}^{(t-t_{N})^{+}}E[X^{N}_{s}(\mbox{\bf 1})]ds.

(7.44)

This implies that for
t\leq t_{N}
,

E[X^{N}_{t}(\mbox{\bf 1})]\leq X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})+2\|r\||\bar{\mathcal{N}}|(\log N)^{3}\int_{0}^{t}E[X^{N}_{s}(\mbox{\bf 1})]ds,

(7.45)

and thus by Gronwall’s inequality, for
t\leq t_{N}
,

E[X^{N}_{t}(\mbox{\bf 1})]\leq\exp(2\|r\||\bar{\mathcal{N}}|(\log N)^{3}t)X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})\leq\exp(2\|r\||\bar{\mathcal{N}}|(\log N)^{-16})X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})\leq e^{2\|r\||\bar{\mathcal{N}}|}X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1}).

(7.46)

By plugging this bound into (
7.44
) we obtain for all

t>0
, (recall
N\geq 3
)

E[X^{N}_{t}(\mbox{\bf 1})]\leq X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})+e^{2\|r\||\bar{\mathcal{N}}|}2\|r\||\bar{\mathcal{N}}|(\log N)^{-16}X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})+C_{\ref{dN3cnd1}}\int_{0}^{t}E[X^{N}_{s}(\mbox{\bf 1})]ds.

Another use of Gronwall’s inequality completes the proof of part (a).
∎

Before proving (b) we establish some preparatory results
which will also be useful later. Let

K_{N}=\sum_{y\in{\mathbb{N}}_{N}}p_{N}(y)\log N\widehat{P}(\sigma^{N}(0,y)>t_{N}).

(7.47)

By Lemma A.3(ii) in
[
6
]
,

\lim_{N\to\infty}K_{N}=2\pi\sigma^{2}.

(7.48)

From (
6.10
) we have

E[X^{N}_{t}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2}]\leq 4\Big[X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2}+E[\langle M^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})\rangle_{t}]+E[D^{N,2}_{t}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2}]+E[D^{N,3}_{t}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2}]\Big].

(7.49)

Recall from (
6.11
) that
\langle M^{N}(\Phi)\rangle_{t}
=
\langle M^{N}(\Phi)\rangle_{1,t}+\langle M^{N}(\Phi)\rangle_{2,t}
.

Lemma 7.7
.

For any
T>0
there is a
constant
C_{\ref{CMPL51b}}>0
, depending on
T
, so that
for any bounded Borel function
\Phi
on
[0,T]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2}

and all
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\langle M^{N}(\Phi)\rangle_{1,t}

\displaystyle=2\int_{0}^{t}X^{N}_{s}((\log N)\Phi_{s}^{2}f^{(N)}_{0}(\cdot,\xi^{N}_{s}))ds+\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{m}^{N}_{1,s}(\Phi)ds

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\text{ where }\displaystyle|\tilde{m}^{N}_{1,s}(\Phi)|\leq C_{\ref{CMPL51b}}\frac{\|\Phi\|^{2}_{\text{{Lip}}}(\log N)}{\sqrt{N}}X^{N}_{s}(\mbox{\bf 1}),

(7.50)

and

\displaystyle\langle M^{N}(\Phi)\rangle_{2,t}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{m}^{N}_{2,s}(\Phi)ds,\ \text{where }|\tilde{m}^{N}_{2,s}(\Phi)|\leq 2\|r\||\bar{\mathcal{N}}|\|\Phi\|_{\infty}^{2}\frac{\ell^{(3)}_{N}}{N^{\prime}}X^{N}_{s}(\mbox{\bf 1})ds.

(7.51)

Proof.

(
7.50
) holds by the corresponding result in
Lemma 4.8 of
[
12
]
. Note that the result there is
actually an identity and bound for a generic state

\xi\in\{0,1\}^{S_{N}}
(although it is not stated as such) and
applies immediately to our setting as well. For
(
7.51
) note first that by their definitions,

|c^{N,a}(x,\xi)|=|d^{N,2}(x,\xi)|
and

|c^{N,s}(x,\xi)|=|d^{N,3}(x,\xi)|
. The second result now
follows immediately by using (
7.4
) to bound the
absolute value of the integrand of the integrals in
(
6.12
) for
\langle M^{N}(\Phi)\rangle_{2,t}
.
∎

Lemma 7.8
.

There is a
C_{\ref{CMPL52}}>0
so that for any bounded
Borel
\Phi
on
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
:

(a)

\frac{\log N}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}\sum_{y\in{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}\Phi(x)p_{N}(y)\hat{E}(\xi_{0}^{N}(B^{N,x}_{t_{N}})1(\sigma^{N}(x,x+y)>t_{N}))=K_{N}X_{0}^{N}(\Phi)+{\mathcal{E}}_{N},

(7.52)

where

|{\mathcal{E}}_{N}|\leq C_{\ref{CMPL52}}|\Phi|_{\text{{Lip}}}(\log N)^{-17/2}X_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1}).

(7.53)

(b) If, in addition,
\Phi\geq 0
, then

E\Big[X^{N}_{t_{N}}((\log N)\Phi f^{(N)}_{0}(\cdot,\xi^{N}_{t_{N}}))\Big]\leq C_{\ref{CMPL52}}\Big(\|\Phi\|_{\text{{Lip}}}(\log N)^{-17/2}X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})+X^{N}_{0}(\Phi)\Big).

(7.54)

Proof.

(a) Let
\Sigma_{N}
denote the left-hand side
of (
7.52
). We may write
B^{N,x}
as
x+B^{N,0}

and sum over the values of
B^{N,0}_{t_{N}}
to see that

\displaystyle\Sigma_{N}

\displaystyle=\frac{\log N}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}\sum_{w\in{S_{N}}}\sum_{y\in{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}p_{N}(y)[\Phi(x)-\Phi(x+w)]\xi_{0}^{N}(x+w)\hat{P}(B_{t_{N}}^{N,0}=w,\sigma^{N}(0,y)>t_{N})

\displaystyle\ \ +\frac{\log N}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}\sum_{w\in{S_{N}}}\Phi(x+w)\xi_{0}^{N}(x+w)\sum_{y\in{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}p_{N}(y)\hat{P}(B_{t_{N}}^{N,0}=w,\sigma^{N}(0,y)>t_{N})

\displaystyle:=\Sigma_{N}^{(1)}+\Sigma_{N}^{(2)}.

Use the implicit Lipschitz continuity of
\Phi
to see that

\displaystyle|\Sigma_{N}^{(1)}|

\displaystyle\leq|\Phi|_{\text{{Lip}}}\sum_{w\in{S_{N}}}|w|\Bigl(\frac{\log N}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}\xi^{N}_{0}(x+w)\Bigr)\sum_{y\in{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}p_{N}(y)\hat{P}(B^{N,0}_{t_{N}}=w,\sigma^{N}(0,y)>t_{N})

\displaystyle\leq|\Phi|_{\text{{Lip}}}X_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})\log N\hat{E}(|B^{N,0}_{t_{N}}|)\leq|\Phi|_{\text{{Lip}}}X_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})\log N\sqrt{2}\sigma\sqrt{t_{N}}.

(7.55)

The fact that
w_{N}\leq 1
is used in the last
line as well. Sum over
x
first and then
w
, to see that

\Sigma^{(2)}_{N}=K_{N}X_{0}^{N}(\Phi)
. This gives (a).

(b) Assume now
\Phi\geq 0
. We will compare with the
corresponding expression for
\xi^{N,{\texttt{vm}}}_{t_{N}}
, and use
the duality (
7.18
) to compute the latter, and hence
see that the left-hand side of (
7.54
) equals

\frac{\log N}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}\sum_{y\in{\mathcal{N}}}\Phi(x)p_{N}(y)E_{\xi^{N}_{0}}[\xi^{N}_{t_{N}}(x)\widehat{\xi}^{N}_{t_{N}}(x+y)-\xi^{N,{\texttt{vm}}}_{t_{N}}(x)\widehat{\xi}^{N,{\texttt{vm}}}_{t_{N}}(x+y)]\\
+\frac{\log N}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}\sum_{y\in{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}\Phi(x)p_{N}(y)\hat{E}[\xi^{N}_{0}(B^{N,x}_{t_{N}})\widehat{\xi}^{N}_{0}(B^{N,x+y}_{t_{N}})].

(7.56)

By the coupling (
7.11
), Lemma
7.1
, and
the triangle inequality, the absolute value of the first sum
in (
7.56
) is bounded by

\displaystyle\|\Phi\|_{\infty}\frac{\log N}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}\sum_{y\in{\mathcal{N}}}p_{N}(y)

\displaystyle\Big(E[\bar{\xi}^{N}_{t_{N}}(x)-{\xi}^{N}_{t_{N}}(x)]+E[\bar{\xi}^{N}_{t_{N}}(x)-\xi^{N,{\texttt{vm}}}_{t_{N}}(x)]

\displaystyle+E[\bar{\xi}^{N}_{t_{N}}(x+y)-{\xi}^{N}_{t_{N}}(x+y)]+E[\bar{\xi}^{N}_{t_{N}}(x+y)-{\xi}^{N.{\texttt{vm}}}_{t_{N}}(x+y)]\Bigr)

\displaystyle=2\|\Phi\|_{\infty}(\log N)\Bigl(E[\bar{X}^{N}_{t_{N}}(\mbox{\bf 1})-{X}^{N}_{t_{N}}(\mbox{\bf 1})]+E[\bar{X}^{N}_{t_{N}}(\mbox{\bf 1})-{X}^{N,{\texttt{vm}}}_{t_{N}}(\mbox{\bf 1})]\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\Phi\|_{\infty}(\log N)^{-15}X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1}).

(7.57)

The second sum in (
7.56
) is bounded by
\Sigma_{N}

(the left-hand side of part (a)), and so (a) and
(
7.48
) give the required bound. ∎

Proof of
Proposition
7.6
(b).
It now
follows quite easily from Lemmas
7.7
and

7.8
(b), as well as part (a), that there is
a constant
C_{\ref{qvbnd1}}
depending on
T
such that,

E[\langle M^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})\rangle_{T}]\leq C_{\ref{qvbnd1}}X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1}).

(7.58)

Here one uses the Markov property and
Lemma
7.8
(b) to bound
\int_{t_{N}}^{T}X^{N}_{s}((\log N)f^{(N)}_{0}(\xi^{N}_{s}))ds
, and the obvious crude
bound on the integrand to handle the integral from
0
to

t_{N}
.

Turning to the last terms in (
7.49
), we consider more
generally
E[(D^{N,j}_{t}(\Phi))^{2}]
, where
\Phi:{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\to{\mathbb{R}}

is Lipschitz continuous. We claim that for

j=2,3
,

E[(D^{N,j}_{t\wedge t_{N}}(\Phi))^{2}]\leq\|\Phi\|^{2}_{\infty}|\bar{\mathcal{N}}|^{2}(\log N)^{6}t_{N}E\Big[\int_{0}^{t\wedge t_{N}}X^{N}_{s}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2}ds\Big],

(7.59)

and there is a constant
C_{\ref{DNt1t2}}>0
such that if

t_{2}>t_{1}\geq t_{N}
, then

E[(D^{N,j}_{t_{2}}(\Phi)-D^{N,j}_{t_{1}}(\Phi))^{2}]\\
\leq C_{\ref{DNt1t2}}\Big(\|\Phi\|_{\text{{Lip}}}^{2}(t_{2}-t_{1})+\|\Phi\|_{\infty}^{2}(\log N)^{6}(t_{N}\wedge(t_{2}-t_{1}))\Big)\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}E[X^{N}_{s}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2}]ds.

(7.60)

The proofs of the corresponding inequalities (66) and (69)
in
[
9
]
apply here without change if (41) and (48)
there are replaced by (
7.39
) and (
7.40
)
here, using
\ell^{(2)}_{N}=\log N\leq(\log N)^{3}
. (The
increment bound is stronger than we need here but will be
useful later in establishing tightness.) Now use
(
7.58
), (
7.59
) and (
7.60
) in
(
7.49
) to see there is a constant
C_{\ref{XN2(1)}}

depending on
T
such that if
t\leq T
then

E[X^{N}_{t}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2}]\leq C_{\ref{XN2(1)}}\Big[X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2}+X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})+\int_{0}^{t}E[X^{N}_{s}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2}]ds\Big].

(7.61)

A simple Gronwall argument finishes the proof of
Proposition
7.6
(b).
∎

7.3
Exact drift asymptotics and first moment measure bounds

Introduce, for
\delta>0
,

\mathscr{I}^{N}(\delta,\xi_{0}^{N})=\int\int 1_{\{|w-z|<\delta\}}dX_{0}^{N}(w)dX_{0}^{N}(z),

(7.62)

and define

\mathscr{I}^{N}(\xi_{0}^{N})=\mathscr{I}^{N}\Big(\sqrt{t_{N}(\log N)^{1/2}},\xi^{N}_{0}\Big).

(7.63)

Lemma 7.9
.

There is a

C_{\ref{2walks}}
so that for
N\geq N(\varepsilon_{0})
, distinct

a_{0},a_{1},a_{2}\in\bar{{\mathcal{N}}}_{N}
, and
\xi^{N}_{0}\in\{0,1\}^{S_{N}}
,

\displaystyle\frac{(\log N)^{3}}{N^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}\widehat{P}\Big(\xi^{N}_{0}(B^{N,x+a_{1}}_{t_{N}})=\xi^{N}_{0}(B^{N,x+a_{2}}_{t_{N}})=1,\sigma^{N}_{x}(a_{0},a_{1},a_{2})>t_{N}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\ref{2walks}}\Bigl(\frac{1}{t_{N}\log N}\mathscr{I}^{N}(\xi^{N}_{0})+(\log N)^{-(1/2)}X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})\Bigr),

(7.64)

and

\displaystyle\frac{(\log N)}{N^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}\widehat{P}\Big(\xi^{N}_{0}(B^{N,x+a_{1}}_{t_{N}})=\xi^{N}_{0}(B^{N,x+a_{2}}_{t_{N}})=1,\sigma^{N}_{x}(a_{1},a_{2})>t_{N}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\ref{2walks}}\Bigl(\frac{1}{t_{N}\log N}\mathscr{I}^{N}(\xi^{N}_{0})+(\log N)^{-(1/2)}X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})\Bigr).

(7.65)

Proof.

The proof of (
7.9
) may be found in
the derivation of (46) in
[
9
]
with
\Phi=\mbox{\bf 1}
. See, in
particular, the bound on
\mathcal{T}_{N}(\Phi)
in that proof
on pages 1214 and 1215, and note we are setting
\eta=1/2

in the final display of Section 4.2 of that article. In
that result the stochastic process
\xi^{N}_{u}(\mbox{\bf 1})
is a
rescaled two-dimensional Lotka-Volterra model but may be
replaced in the proof by a fixed point

\xi^{N}_{0}\in\{0,1\}^{S_{N}}
and so holds equally well in our
setting. The derivation of (
7.9
) is the same. One
just replaces
\sigma_{x}^{N}(a_{0},a_{1},a_{2})
with

\sigma_{x}^{N}(a_{1},a_{2})
. Note that (
1.7
) is used in
the proofs with
n=3
and
n=2
, respectively.
∎

For nonempty sets
A\subset\bar{\mathcal{N}}
define

\displaystyle\Theta^{N}_{2}(A)

\displaystyle=(\log N)\widehat{P}(\sigma^{N}(A/\sqrt{N},(\bar{\mathcal{N}}\setminus A)/\sqrt{N})>t_{N},\,\tau^{N}(A/\sqrt{N},(\bar{\mathcal{N}}\setminus A)/\sqrt{N})<t_{N})

\displaystyle=(\log N)\widehat{P}(\sigma(A,\bar{\mathcal{N}}\setminus A)>Nw_{N}t_{N},\,\tau(A,\bar{\mathcal{N}}\setminus A)<Nw_{N}t_{N})

\displaystyle\Theta^{N}_{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\emptyset\neq A\subset{\mathcal{N}}}r^{N,a}(A)\Theta^{N}_{2}(A).

By Proposition
1.6
and (
3.28
),

\lim_{N\to\infty}\Theta^{N}_{2}=\Theta_{2}.

(7.66)

Proposition 7.10
.

There is a

C_{\ref{^HTht2}}>0
such
that for any
T>0
,
\phi\in C_{b}([0,T]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2})
,
and
0\leq s,u\leq T
with
|u-s|\leq t_{N}
,

\Big|\hat{H}^{N,2}(\xi^{N}_{0},t_{N},\Phi_{s})-\Theta^{N}_{2}X^{N}_{0}(\Phi_{u})\Big|\leq C_{\ref{^HTht2}}\|\Phi\|\Bigl(\frac{1}{t_{N}\log N}\mathscr{I}^{N}(\xi^{N}_{0})+(\log N)^{-1/2}X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})\Bigr).

(7.67)

Proof.

In view of Lemma
7.4
we will start with the
difference
\hat{H}^{N,2}_{2}(\xi^{N}_{0},t_{N},\Phi_{s})-\Theta^{N}_{2}X^{N}_{0}(\Phi_{s})
.
Let
\emptyset\neq A\subset{\mathcal{N}}_{N}
, and recall the definition
of
\Omega^{N}_{x}(A)
in (
7.36
), taking
u_{N}=t_{N}
.
For any fixed
a\in A
,

I^{N,+}_{2}(x,t_{N},A,\xi^{N}_{0})=\big[\xi^{N}_{0}(B^{N,x+a}_{t_{N}})-\xi^{N}_{0}(B^{N,x+a}_{t_{N}})\xi^{N}_{0}(B^{N,x}_{t_{N}})\big]1\{\Omega^{N}_{x}(A)\},

(7.68)

and thus

|\hat{E}\big(I^{N,+}_{2}(x,t_{N},A,\xi^{N}_{0})\big)-\hat{P}\big(B^{N,x+a}_{t_{N}}\in\xi^{N}_{0},\Omega^{N}_{x}(A)\big)|\\
\leq\hat{P}\big(B^{N,x+a}_{t_{N}}\in\xi^{N}_{0},B^{N,x}_{t_{N}}\in\xi^{N}_{0},\sigma^{N}(x+a,x)>t_{N}\big).

(7.69)

If we choose an
a=a(A)\in A
for each non-empty

A\subset{\mathcal{N}}_{N}
, define

\tilde{H}^{N,2}_{2}(\xi^{N}_{0},t_{N},\Phi_{s})=\sum_{\emptyset\neq A\subset{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}r^{N,a}(\sqrt{N}A)\frac{\log N}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}\Phi(s,x)\hat{P}(B^{N,x+a}_{t_{N}}\in\xi^{N}_{0},\Omega^{N}_{x}(A)).

(7.70)

It then follows from Proposition
7.9
that for a
constant
C

|\hat{H}^{N,2}_{2}(\xi^{N}_{0},t_{N},\Phi_{s})-\tilde{H}^{N,2}_{2}(\xi^{N}_{0},t_{N},\Phi_{s})|\leq C\|\Phi\|_{\infty}\Bigl(\frac{1}{t_{N}\log N}\mathscr{I}^{N}(\xi^{N}_{0})+(\log N)^{-(1/2)}X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})\Bigr).

(7.71)

To evaluate
\tilde{H}^{N,2}_{t_{N}}
, for fixed
A
we have

\displaystyle\frac{\log N}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}

\displaystyle\Phi(s,x)\hat{P}(B^{N,x+a}\in\xi^{N}_{0},\Omega^{N}_{x}(A))

\displaystyle=\frac{\log N}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}\Phi(s,x)\sum_{w\in{S_{N}}}\xi^{N}_{0}(x+w)\hat{P}(B^{N,x+a}_{t_{N}}=x+w,\Omega^{N}_{x}(A))

\displaystyle=\frac{\log N}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}\sum_{w\in{S_{N}}}[\Phi(s,x)-\Phi(s,x+w)]\xi^{N}_{0}(x+w)\hat{P}(B^{N,a}_{t_{N}}=w,\Omega^{N}_{0}(A))

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\log N}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}\sum_{w\in{S_{N}}}\Phi(s,x+w)\xi^{N}_{0}(x+w)\hat{P}(B^{N,a}_{t_{N}}=w,\Omega^{N}_{0}(A)).

As in the proof of (
7.33
) (see
(
7.37
)),

\frac{\log N}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}\sum_{w\in{S_{N}}}|\Phi(s,x)-\Phi(s,x+w)|\xi^{N}_{0}(x+w)\hat{P}(B^{N,a}_{t_{N}}=w)\\
\leq C({\mathcal{N}})\|\Phi_{s}\|_{\text{{Lip}}}X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})(\log N)^{-17/2}.

(7.72)

Summing first over
x
and then over
w
,

\frac{\log N}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}\sum_{w\in{S_{N}}}\Phi(s,x+w)\xi^{N}_{0}(x+w)\hat{P}(B^{N,0}_{t_{N}}=w,\Omega^{N}_{0}(A))=X^{N}_{0}(\Phi_{s})(\log N)\hat{P}(\Omega^{N}_{0}(A)).

(7.73)

Combining (
7.71
)–(
7.73
), summing over

\emptyset\neq A\subset{\mathcal{N}}
, and using the
definition of
\Theta^{N,2}

we get

\displaystyle|\hat{H}^{N,2}_{2}(\xi^{N}_{0},

\displaystyle t_{N},\Phi_{s})-\Theta^{N}_{2}X^{N}_{0}(\Phi_{s})|

(7.74)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\Phi\|\Bigl(\frac{1}{t_{N}\log N}\mathscr{I}^{N}(\xi^{N}_{0})+(\log N)^{-(1/2)}X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})\Bigr).

Now combine this with the easy bound
|X^{N}_{0}(\Phi(s,\cdot)-X^{N}_{0}(\Phi(u,\cdot)|\leq|\Phi|_{1/2,N}\sqrt{t_{N}}X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})

and the boundedness of
\{\Theta^{N}_{2}\}
to derive
(
7.67
), but with
\hat{H}^{N,2}_{2}
in place of
\hat{H}^{N,2}
. Finally, use (
7.31
) for
i\geq 3
and the
above bound in the representation (
7.30
) for
\hat{H}^{N,2}
to complete the proof.
∎

The proof of Proposition
7.11
below, the analogue
of Proposition
7.10
for
\hat{H}^{N,3}
, is more
involved and requires additional notation.
For nonempty finite disjoint sets
A,A_{1},A_{2}\subset\bar{\mathcal{N}}

let

\displaystyle\Theta^{N}_{3}

\displaystyle(A,A_{1},A_{2})

\displaystyle=(\log N)^{3}\widehat{P}(\sigma^{N}(A/\sqrt{N},A_{1}/\sqrt{N},A_{2}/\sqrt{N})>t_{N},\,\tau^{N}(A/\sqrt{N},A_{1}/\sqrt{N},A_{2}/\sqrt{N})<t_{N})

\displaystyle=(\log N)^{3}\widehat{P}(\sigma(A,A_{1},A_{2})>Nw_{N}t_{N},\,\tau(A,A_{1},A_{2})<Nw_{N}t_{N}),

and define

\displaystyle\Theta^{N,+}_{3}(A)=\sum_{\{A_{1},A_{2}\}\in{\mathcal{P}}(\bar{\mathcal{N}}\setminus A)}\Theta^{N}_{3}(A,A_{1},A_{2}),

\displaystyle\Theta^{N,-}_{3}(A)=\sum_{\{A_{1},A_{2}\}\in{\mathcal{P}}(A)}\Theta^{N}_{3}(\bar{\mathcal{N}}\setminus A,A_{1},A_{2}),

\displaystyle\Theta^{N\pm}_{3}=\sum_{\emptyset\neq A\subset{\mathcal{N}}}r^{N,s}(A)\Theta^{N,\pm}(A)

(7.75)

and

\Theta^{N}_{3}=\Theta^{N,+}_{3}-\Theta^{N,-}_{3}
. Proposition
1.6

and (
3.30
) imply that if
\Theta_{3}
is as in
(
3.33
), then

\lim_{N}\Theta^{N}_{3}=\Theta_{3}.

(7.76)

Proposition 7.11
.

There is a constant

C_{\ref{^HTht}}>0
such
that for any
T>0
,
\phi\in C_{b}([0,T]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2})
,
and
0\leq s,u\leq T
with
|u-s|\leq t_{N}
,

\Big|\hat{H}^{N,3}(\xi^{N}_{0},t_{N},\Phi_{s})-\Theta^{N}_{3}X^{N}_{0}(\Phi_{u})\Big|\leq C_{\ref{^HTht}}\|\Phi\|\Bigl(\frac{1}{t_{N}\log N}\mathscr{I}^{N}(\xi^{N}_{0})+(\log N)^{-1/2}X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})\Bigr).

(7.77)

Proof.

Let us start with
the difference

\hat{H}^{N}_{3}(\xi^{N}_{0},t_{N},\Phi_{s})-\Theta^{N}X^{N}_{0}(\Phi(u,\cdot))
. Assume
\emptyset\neq A\subset{\mathcal{N}}_{N}
.
It is easy to see that if

I^{N}_{3}(x,t_{N},A,\xi^{N}_{0})\neq 0
, then exactly one of

|B^{N,A}_{t_{N}}|
or
|B^{N,\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}\setminus A}_{t_{N}}|
must
be 2, while the other must be 1.
To account for these possibilities, we introduce

\displaystyle\chi^{N,+}_{1}(x,t_{N},A,\xi^{N}_{0})

\displaystyle=1\{B^{N,x+A}_{t_{N}}\subset\xi^{N}_{0},|B^{N,x+A}_{t_{N}}|=2,B^{N,x+\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}\setminus A}_{t_{N}}\subset\widehat{\xi}^{N}_{0},|B^{N,x+\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}\setminus A}_{t_{N}}|=1\},

\displaystyle\chi^{N,-}_{1}(x,t_{N},A,\xi^{N}_{0})

\displaystyle=1\{B^{N,x+A}_{t_{N}}\subset\widehat{\xi}^{N}_{0},|B^{N,x+A}_{t_{N}}|=1,B^{N,x+\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}\setminus A}_{t_{N}}\subset\xi^{N}_{0},|B^{N,x+\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}\setminus A}_{t_{N}}|=2\},

\displaystyle\chi^{N,+}_{2}(x,t_{N},A,\xi^{N}_{0})

\displaystyle=1\{B^{N,x+A}_{t_{N}}\subset\xi^{N}_{0},|B^{N,x+A}_{t_{N}}|=1,B^{N,x+\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}\setminus A}_{t_{N}}\subset\widehat{\xi}^{N}_{0},|B^{N,x+\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}\setminus A}_{t_{N}}|=2\},

\displaystyle\chi^{N,-}_{2}(x,t_{N},A,\xi^{N}_{0})

\displaystyle=1\{B^{N,x+A}_{t_{N}}\subset\widehat{\xi}^{N}_{0},|B^{N,x+A}_{t_{N}}|=2,B^{N,x+\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}\setminus A}_{t_{N}}\subset\xi^{N}_{0},|B^{N,x+\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}\setminus A}_{t_{N}}|=1\},

and for
i=1,2
,

\displaystyle\hat{\Sigma}^{N,i}_{3}

\displaystyle(\xi^{N}_{0},t_{N},\Phi_{s})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\emptyset\neq A\subsetneq{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}r^{N,s}(\sqrt{N}A)\frac{(\log N)^{3}}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}\Phi(s,x)\widehat{E}[\chi_{i}^{N,+}(x,t_{N},A,\xi^{N}_{0})-\chi_{i}^{N,-}(x,t_{N},A,\xi^{N}_{0})].

It follows that

(I^{N,+}_{3}-I^{N,-}_{3})(x,t_{N},A,\xi^{N}_{0})=(\chi^{N,+}_{1}-\chi^{N,-}_{1})(x,t_{N},A,\xi^{N}_{0})+(\chi^{N,+}_{2}-\chi^{N,-}_{2})(x,t_{N},A,\xi^{N}_{0}),

and thus

\hat{H}^{N}_{3}(\xi^{N}_{0},t_{N},\Phi_{s})=\hat{\Sigma}^{N,1}_{3}(\xi^{N}_{0},t_{N},\Phi_{s})+\hat{\Sigma}^{N,2}_{3}(\xi^{N}_{0},t_{N},\Phi_{s}).

(7.78)

The term
\hat{\Sigma}^{N,1}_{3}(\xi^{N}_{0},t_{N},\Phi_{s})
is
small. This is because
both
\chi^{N,+}_{1}
and
\chi^{N,-}_{1}
are bounded above by

\sum_{\text{distinct }a_{0},a_{1},a_{2}\in\bar{\mathcal{N}}}1\{B^{N,x+a_{1}}_{t_{N}}\in\xi^{N}_{0},B^{N,x+a_{2}}_{t_{N}}\in\xi^{N}_{0},\sigma^{N}_{x}(a_{0},a_{1},a_{2})>t_{N}\},

which implies by Lemma
7.9
that

|\hat{\Sigma}^{N,1}_{3}(\xi^{N}_{0},t_{N},\Phi_{s})|\leq C_{\ref{2walks}}\binom{|\bar{\mathcal{N}}|}{3}\|\Phi\|_{\infty}\Bigl(\frac{1}{t_{N}\log N}\mathscr{I}^{N}(\xi^{N}_{0})+(\log N)^{-1/2}X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})\Bigr).

(7.79)

To handle
\hat{\Sigma}^{N,2}_{3}
it is convenient to define

\displaystyle\hat{\Sigma}^{N,2,\pm}_{3}(\xi^{N}_{0},t_{N},\Phi_{s})=\sum_{\emptyset\neq A\subset{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}r^{N,s}(\sqrt{N}A)\frac{(\log N)^{3}}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}\Phi(s,x)\widehat{E}\big(\chi_{2}^{N,\pm}(x,t_{N},A,\xi^{N}_{0})\big),

so that

\hat{\Sigma}^{N,2}_{3}(\xi_{0}^{N},t_{N},\Phi_{s})=\hat{\Sigma}^{N,2,+}_{3}(\xi_{0}^{N},t_{N},\Phi_{s})-\hat{\Sigma}^{N,2,-}_{3}(\xi_{0}^{N},t_{N},\Phi_{s}).

(7.80)

Consider
\hat{\Sigma}^{N,2,+}_{3}
, and let

\emptyset\neq A\subset{\mathcal{N}}_{N}
.
On the event defining

\chi^{N,+}_{2}(x,t_{N},A,\xi^{N}_{0})
there must
exist nonempty disjoint
A_{1},A_{2}
whose union is

\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}\setminus A
,
such that none of the walks starting from the three disjoint sets
A,A_{1}
and
A_{2}

meet by time
t_{N}
,

|B^{N,x+A_{1}}_{t_{N}}|=|B^{N,x+A_{2}}_{t_{N}}|=1
and

B^{N,x+A_{1}}_{t_{N}},B^{N,x+A_{2}}_{t_{N}}\subset\widehat{\xi}^{N}_{0}
.
Thus, if we define

\Omega^{N}_{x}(A,A_{1},A_{2})=\{\sigma^{N}_{x}(A,A_{1},A_{2})>t_{N},\tau^{N}_{x}(A,A_{1},A_{2})<t_{N}\},

then for any

a\in A
and
a_{i}\in A_{i}
,

\widehat{E}\big(\chi^{N,+}_{2}(x,t_{N},A,\xi^{N}_{0})\big)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\{A_{1},A_{2}\}\in\\
{\mathcal{P}}(\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}\setminus A)\end{subarray}}\widehat{P}\Big(\Omega^{N}_{x}(A,A_{1},A_{2}),B^{N,x+a}_{t_{N}}\in\xi^{N}_{0},B^{N,x+a_{1}}_{t_{N}}\in\widehat{\xi}^{N}_{0},B^{N,x+a_{2}}_{t_{N}}\in\widehat{\xi}^{N}_{0}\Big).

(7.81)

The next step is to see that we may drop requirement that

B^{N,x+a_{1}}_{t_{N}},B^{N,x+a_{2}}\in\widehat{\xi}^{N}_{0}
above. Observe
that

\displaystyle\Big|\widehat{P}\Big(

\displaystyle\Omega^{N}_{x}(A,A_{1},A_{2}),B^{N,x+a}_{t_{N}}\in\xi^{N}_{0},B^{N,x+a_{1}}_{t_{N}}\in\widehat{\xi}^{N}_{0},B^{N,x+a_{2}}_{t_{N}}\in\widehat{\xi}^{N}_{0}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-\widehat{P}\Big(\Omega^{N}_{x}(A,A_{1},A_{2}),B^{N,x+a}_{t_{N}}\in\xi^{N}_{0}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\widehat{P}\Big(\sigma^{N}_{x}(A,A_{1},A_{2})>t_{N},B^{N,x+a}_{t_{N}}\in\xi^{N}_{0},B^{N,x+a_{1}}_{t_{N}}\text{ or }B^{N,x+a_{2}}_{t_{N}}\in\xi^{N}_{0}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq 2\max_{\text{distinct }a,a_{1},a_{2}\in\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}\widehat{P}(\sigma^{N}_{x}(a,a_{1},a_{2})>t_{N},B^{N,x+a}_{t_{N}}\in\xi^{N}_{0},B^{N,x+a_{1}}_{t_{N}}\in\xi^{N}_{0}).

(7.82)

If we let

\widetilde{\Sigma}^{N,2,+}_{3}(\xi^{N}_{0},t_{N},\Phi_{s})\\
=\sum_{A\subset{\mathcal{N}}}r^{N,s}(\sqrt{N}A)\frac{(\log N)^{3}}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}\Phi(s,x)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\{A_{1},A_{2}\}\in\\
{\mathcal{P}}(\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}\setminus A)\end{subarray}}\widehat{P}\Big(\Omega^{N}_{x}(A,A_{1},A_{2}),B^{N,x+a}_{t_{N}}\in\xi^{N}_{0}\Big),

(7.83)

it follows from the above and Lemma
7.9
that
there is a constant
C_{\ref{e:+3}}
such that

\Big|\hat{\Sigma}^{N,2,+}_{3}(\xi^{N}_{0},t_{N},\Phi_{s})-\widetilde{\Sigma}^{N,2,+}_{3}(\xi^{N}_{0},t_{N},\Phi_{s})\Big|\\
\leq C_{\ref{e:+3}}\|\Phi\|_{\infty}\Bigl(\frac{1}{t_{N}\log N}\mathscr{I}^{N}(\xi^{N}_{0})+(\log N)^{-1/2}X_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})\Bigr).

(7.84)

Again, consider a fixed
\emptyset\neq A\subset{\mathcal{N}}_{N}
and
let
\{A_{1},A_{2}\}\in{\mathcal{P}}(\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}\setminus A)
. By
translation invariance,

\displaystyle\frac{(\log N)^{3}}{N^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}\Phi(s,x)\widehat{P}\Big(\Omega^{N}_{x}(A,A_{1},A_{2}),B^{N,x+a}_{t_{N}}\in\xi^{N}_{0})

\displaystyle=\frac{(\log N)^{3}}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}\sum_{w\in{S_{N}}}\Phi(s,x)\xi^{N}_{0}(x+w)\widehat{P}\Big(\Omega^{N}_{x}(A,A_{1},A_{2}),B^{N,x+a}_{t_{N}}=x+w\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{(\log N)^{3}}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}\sum_{w\in{S_{N}}}[\Phi(s,x)-\Phi(s,x+w)]\xi^{N}_{0}(x+w)\widehat{P}\Big(\Omega^{N}_{0}(A,A_{1},A_{2}),B^{N,a}_{t_{N}}=w\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{(\log N)^{3}}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}\sum_{w\in{S_{N}}}\Phi(s,x+w)\xi^{N}_{0}(x+w)\widehat{P}\Big(\Omega^{N}_{0}(A,A_{1},A_{2}),B^{N,a}_{t_{N}}=w\Big).

(7.85)

As in the proof of (
7.33
) (see
(
7.37
)),

\frac{(\log N)^{3}}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}\sum_{w\in{S_{N}}}\Big|(\Phi(s,x)-\Phi(s,x+w))\Big|\xi^{N}_{0}(x+w)\widehat{P}\Big(\Omega^{N}_{0}(A,A_{1},A_{2}),B^{N,a}_{t_{N}}=w\Big)\\
\leq C_{\ref{e:+5}}\|\Phi\|_{\text{{Lip}}}X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})(\log N)^{-13/2}.

(7.86)

For the final sum in (
7.3
), summing first over
x

and then over
w
yields

\frac{(\log N)^{3}}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}\sum_{w\in{S_{N}}}\Phi(s,x+w)\xi^{N}_{0}(x+w)\widehat{P}\Big(\Omega^{N}_{0}(A,A_{1},A_{2}),B^{N,a}_{t_{N}}=w\Big)\\
=\Theta^{N}_{3}(\sqrt{N}A,\sqrt{N}A_{1},\sqrt{N}A_{2})X^{N}_{0}(\Phi_{s}).

(7.87)

Combining (
7.3
)–(
7.87
) and using the
definition of
\tilde{\Sigma}
we find that

\Big|\frac{(\log N)^{3}}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}\Phi(s,x)\widehat{P}\Big(\Omega^{N}_{x}(A,A_{1},A_{2}),B^{N,x+a}_{t_{N}}\in\xi^{N}_{0}\Big)-\Theta^{N}_{3}(\sqrt{N}A,\sqrt{N}A_{1},\sqrt{N}A_{2})X^{N}_{0}(\Phi_{s})\Big|\\
\leq C_{\ref{e:+5}}\|\Phi\|_{\text{{Lip}}}X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})(\log N)^{-13/2},

(7.88)

\Big|\tilde{\Sigma}^{N,2,+}(\xi^{N}_{0},t_{N},\Phi_{s})-\Theta^{N,+}X^{N}_{0}(\Phi_{s})\Big|\\
\leq C_{\ref{e:+5}}\binom{|\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}|}{3}(\log N)^{-13/2}\|\Phi\|_{\text{{Lip}}}X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1}),

(7.89)

and by (
7.84
),

\Big|\hat{\Sigma}^{N,2,+}_{3}(\xi^{N}_{0},t_{N},\Phi_{s})-\Theta^{N,+}X^{N}_{0}(\Phi_{s})\Big|\\
\leq C_{\ref{SigN23+bnd}}\|\Phi\|_{\text{{Lip}}}\Bigl(\frac{1}{t_{N}\log N}\mathscr{I}^{N}(\xi^{N}_{0})+(\log N)^{-1/2}X_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})\Bigr).

(7.90)

In the above we have rescaled the sets and summed over
subsets of
{\mathcal{N}}
(or
\bar{\mathcal{N}}
) instead of
{\mathcal{N}}_{N}
(or
\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}
). The same bound holds for the difference

\hat{\Sigma}^{N,2,-}_{3}(\xi^{N}_{0},t_{N},\Phi_{s})-\Theta^{N,-}X^{N}_{0}(\Phi_{s})
, and thus in view of (
7.78
),
(
7.79
), (
7.80
), (
7.90
),
and the simple bound
|X^{N}_{0}(\Phi(s,\cdot)-X^{N}_{0}(\Phi(u,\cdot)|\leq|\Phi|_{1/2,N}\sqrt{t_{N}}X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})
, we conclude that

\Big|\hat{H}_{3}^{N}(\xi^{N}_{0},t_{N},\Phi_{s})-\Theta^{N}X^{N}_{0}(\Phi_{u})\Big|\leq C_{\ref{^HTht}}\|\Phi\|\Bigl(\frac{1}{t_{N}\log N}\mathscr{I}^{N}(\xi^{N}_{0})+(\log N)^{-1/2}X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})\Bigr).

Combine this with Lemma
7.4
and
(
7.30
) to obtain (
7.77
).
∎

As an immediate consequence of Lemma
7.3
(with

u_{N}=t_{N}
), Proposition
7.10
and
Proposition
7.11
we obtain the following result.

Proposition 7.12
.

There is a
constant
C_{\ref{dsThtX}}>0
such that for
j=2,3
, any

T>0
,
\Phi\in C_{b}([0,T]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2})
, and
s\in[t_{N},T]
,

\Big|E\big(d^{N,j}(s,\xi^{N}_{s},\Phi)|{\mathcal{F}}^{N}_{s-t_{N}}\big)-\Theta^{N}_{j}X^{N}_{s-t_{N}}(\Phi)\Big|\\
\leq C_{\ref{dsThtX}}\|\Phi\|\Bigl(\frac{1}{t_{N}\log N}\mathscr{I}^{N}(\xi^{N}_{s-t_{N}})+(\log N)^{-1/2}X_{s-t_{N}}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})\Bigr).

(7.91)

Turning briefly to the martingale square function, and recalling (
7.50
) and the definition of
K_{N}
from (
7.47
), we have the following analogue of the above.

Proposition 7.13
.

There is a
C_{\ref{dssqfn}}

such that for any bounded Lipschitz continuous

\Phi:{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\to{\mathbb{R}}
and
s\geq t_{N}
,

\Big|E\big(X^{N}_{s}(\log N\Phi f_{0}^{(N)}(\cdot,\xi^{N}_{s}))|{\mathcal{F}}^{N}_{s-t_{N}}\big)-K_{N}X^{N}_{s-t_{N}}(\Phi)\Big|\\
\leq C_{\ref{dssqfn}}\|\Phi\|_{\text{{Lip}}}\Bigl(\frac{1}{t_{N}\log N}\mathscr{I}^{N}(\xi^{N}_{s-t_{N}})+(\log N)^{-1/2}X_{s-t_{N}}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})\Bigr).

(7.92)

Proof.

By (
7.56
) and (
7.57
),

\displaystyle E(X^{N}_{t_{N}}(\log N\Phi f_{0}^{(N)}(\cdot,\xi^{N}_{t_{N}}))

\displaystyle=\frac{\log N}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}\sum_{y\in{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}\Phi(x)p_{N}(y)\hat{E}(\xi^{N}_{0}(B^{N,x}_{t_{N}})\widehat{\xi}_{0}^{N}(B^{N,x+y}_{t_{N}}))+R_{N}

(7.93)

\displaystyle:=S_{N}+R_{N},

where

|R_{N}|\leq C\|\Phi\|_{\infty}(\log N)^{-15}X_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1}).

(7.94)

Clearly

\displaystyle S_{N}

\displaystyle=\frac{\log N}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}\sum_{y\in{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}\Phi(x)p_{N}(y)\hat{E}(\xi^{N}_{0}(B^{N,x}_{t_{N}})1(\sigma^{N}(x,x+y)>t_{N}))

\displaystyle\ \ -\frac{\log N}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}\sum_{y\in{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}\Phi(x)p_{N}(y)\hat{E}(\xi^{N}_{0}(B^{N,x}_{t_{N}})\xi_{0}^{N}(B^{N,x+y}_{t_{N}})1(\sigma^{N}(x,x+y)>t_{N})

\displaystyle:=S_{N}^{1}-S_{N}^{2}.

(
7.9
) shows that

|S^{2}_{N}|\leq C_{\ref{2walks}}\|\Phi\|_{\infty}\Bigl(\frac{1}{t_{N}\log N}\mathscr{I}^{N}(\xi^{N}_{0})+(\log N)^{-(1/2)}X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})\Bigr).

(7.95)

By Lemma
7.8
(a),

S^{1}_{N}=K_{N}X_{0}^{N}(\Phi)+{\mathcal{E}}_{N},

where

|{\mathcal{E}}_{N}|\leq C_{\ref{CMPL52}}|\Phi|_{\text{{Lip}}}(\log N)^{-17/2}X_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1}).

(7.96)

Use the error bounds (
7.94
), (
7.95
) and
(
7.96
) in the decomposition (
7.93
), and then
apply the Markov property to complete the proof.
∎

In view of the above results, to obtain exact asymptotics
for the limiting drift arising from the
q
-voter
perturbation term as well as the square function of the
martingale term, we will show that
\frac{1}{t_{N}\log N}E\left[\int_{t_{N}}^{t\vee t_{N}}\mathscr{I}^{N}(\xi^{N}_{s-t_{N}})ds\right]
is negligible for large

N
.

Proposition 7.14
.

Let
(\delta_{N})_{N\geq 3}
be a positive sequence such that
\lim_{N\to\infty}\delta_{N}=0
and

\liminf_{N\to\infty}\sqrt{N}\delta_{N}>0
. For any
T
, there exists

C_{\ref{key2bnd}}
, depending on
T
and
\{\delta_{N}\}
,
such that for any
t\leq T
, for any
N\geq 3
,

E[\mathscr{I}^{N}(\delta_{N},\xi^{N}_{t})]\leq C_{\ref{key2bnd}}\Big(X_{0}^{N}(1)+X_{0}^{N}(1)^{2}\Big)\Big[\delta_{N}\Big(1+\log\Big(1+\frac{t}{\delta_{N}}\Big)\Big)+\frac{\delta_{N}}{t+\delta_{N}}\Big].

(7.97)

The proof of this key result is similar in outline to that
of the same result, Proposition 3.10 in
[
9
]
, for
Lotka-Volterra models, although additional complications
will arise in the present setting. We will prove it in
Section
9
below but assume it in what
follows.

Corollary 7.15
.

If
T>0
there exists a constant

C_{\ref{keyint}}
depending on
T
such that for any
t\leq T
,

\frac{1}{t_{N}\log N}E\left[\int_{t_{N}}^{t\vee t_{N}}\mathscr{I}^{N}(\xi^{N}_{s-t_{N}})ds\right]\leq C_{\ref{keyint}}(\log N)^{-1/2}(X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})+X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2}).

(7.98)

Proof.

Given Proposition
7.14
, the
elementary proof of the above is identical to that of
Corollary 3.11 in
[
9
]
. (The bound in the final line
of the proof there leads to an upper bound as above but
instead with
(\log N)^{-\eta+\delta}
for any
\delta>0

and
\eta\in(0,1)
, and hence, in particular,
(\log N)^{-1/2}
).∎

We are finally ready to give the asymptotic behavior of the
drifts,
D^{N,j}_{t}(\Phi)
,
j=2,3
, and so prove the main
result of this Section.

Proposition 7.16
.

For
j=2,3
, any

\Phi:{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\to{\mathbb{R}}
with
\|\Phi\|_{\text{{Lip}}}<\infty
and all
t>0
,

\lim_{N\to\infty}E\Bigl(\Bigl|D^{N,j}_{t}(\Phi)-\Theta_{j}\int_{0}^{t}X_{s}^{N}(\Phi)\,ds\Bigr|\Bigr)=0.

Proof.

Let
t\in(0,T]
. A little thought shows the above expectation is bounded by

\displaystyle E\Bigl(

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t_{N}\wedge t}|d^{N,j}(s,\xi^{N}_{s},\Phi)|\,ds\Bigr)+E\Bigl(\Bigl(\int_{t_{N}}^{t_{N}\vee t}d^{N,j}(s,\xi^{N}_{s},\Phi)-E(d^{N,j}(s,\xi^{N}_{s},\Phi)|{\mathcal{F}}^{N}_{s-t_{N}})\,ds\Bigr)^{2}\Bigr)^{1/2}

\displaystyle+E\Bigl(\int_{t_{N}}^{t_{N}\vee t}|E(d^{N,j}(s,\xi^{N}_{s},\Phi)|{\mathcal{F}}^{N}_{s-t_{N}})-\Theta^{N}_{j}X_{s-t_{N}}^{N}(\Phi)|\,ds\Bigr)

(7.99)

\displaystyle+|\Theta^{N}_{j}-\Theta_{j}|\|\Phi\|_{\infty}E\Bigl(\int_{0}^{(t-t_{N})^{+}}X_{s}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})\,ds\Bigr)+|\Theta_{j}|\|\Phi\|_{\infty}E\Bigl(\int_{(t-t_{N})^{+}}^{t}X_{s}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})\,ds\Bigr).

Proposition
7.6
(a) and (
7.39
) shows the
first term is at most

C\|\Phi\|_{\infty}(\log N)^{3}t_{N}X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})\to 0\text{ as
}N\to\infty.

Use an orthogonality argument as in the
derivation of (67) in
[
9
]
and also (
7.39
) to
bound the second term in (
7.99
) by

C\|\Phi\|_{\infty}(\log N)^{3}t_{N}^{1/2}\Bigl[E\Bigl(\int_{t_{N}}^{t_{N}\vee t}X^{N}_{s}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2}\,ds\Bigr)\Bigr]^{1/2}\leq C_{T}\|\Phi\|_{\infty}(\log N)^{-13/2}(1+X_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1}))\to 0\text{ as
}N\to\infty,

where the inequality holds by
Proposition
7.6
(b). Since

|\Theta^{N}_{j}-\Theta_{j}|\to 0
(by (
7.66
) and
(
7.76
)), we may apply Proposition
7.6
(a)
to see that the last two terms in (
7.99
) are
bounded by

C_{T}\|\Phi\|_{\infty}X_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})[|\Theta^{N}_{j}-\Theta_{j}|+t_{N}]\to 0\text{ as }N\to\infty.

Finally we may use Proposition
7.12
,
Corollary
7.15
and Proposition
7.6
(a)
to bound the remaining (middle) term of
(
7.99
) by

C_{T}\|\Phi\|_{\text{{Lip}}}(\log N)^{-1/2}(X_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})+X_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2})\to 0\text{ as }N\to\infty.

Combining the above displays, we may complete the proof.
∎

Remark 7.17
.

If we keep track of the bounds
in the above proof (and drop the

|\Theta^{N}_{j}-\Theta_{j}|
term) we get for
j=2,3
,

E\Bigl(\Bigl|D^{N,j}_{t}(\Phi)-\Theta^{N}_{j}\int_{0}^{t}X_{s}^{N}(\Phi)\,ds\Bigr|\Bigr)\leq C_{T}\|\Phi\|_{\text{{Lip}}}(1+X_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2})(\log N)^{-1/2}\quad\text{for all }t\in[0,T].

(7.100)

Proposition 7.18
.

For any
\Phi:{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\to{\mathbb{R}}
with
\|\Phi\|_{\text{{Lip}}}<\infty
and all

t>0
,

\lim_{N\to\infty}E\Bigl(\Bigl|\langle M^{N}(\Phi)\rangle_{t}-4\pi\sigma^{2}\int_{0}^{t}X_{s}^{N}(\Phi^{2})\,ds\Bigr|\Bigr)=0.

Proof.

Note that
\|\Phi\|_{\text{{Lip}}}
finite implies that

\|\Phi^{2}\|_{\text{{Lip}}}
is finite. Arguing exactly as in the proof
of Proposition
7.16
, using
Proposition
7.13
in place of
Proposition
7.12
and (
7.48
) in
place of (
7.66
) and (
7.76
), we get

\lim_{N\to\infty}E\bigl(\Big|\int_{0}^{t}X_{s}^{N}(2\log N\Phi^{2}f_{0}^{(N)}(\cdot,\xi^{N}_{s}))\,ds-\int_{0}^{t}4\pi\sigma^{2}X^{N}_{s}(\Phi^{2})\,ds\Big|\Bigr)=0.

Now use (
7.50
), (
7.51
), and
Proposition
7.6
(a) to complete the proof.
∎

Define
(P^{N}_{t},t\geq 0)
as the semigroup of the rate-
N
random
walk on
{S_{N}}
with jump kernel
p_{N}
. By translation invariance we can
have
P^{N}_{t}
operate on functions on the plane, even though
{S_{N}}
is the natural state space.

As an application of the control of the drift terms

d^{N,j}
given by Proposition
7.16
, we obtain an
effective bound on the mean measures of our rescaled

q
-voter models.

Lemma 7.19
.

There exists a

c_{\ref{firstmomeasbnd}}>0
, and for any
T>0
a constant

C_{\ref{firstmomeasbnd}}(T)>0
so that for all
t\in[0,T]

and any
\Psi:{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\to{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
such that
\|\Psi\|_{\text{{Lip}}}\leq T
,

E[X^{N}_{t}(\Psi)]\leq e^{c_{\ref{firstmomeasbnd}}t}X^{N}_{0}(P^{N}_{t}(\Psi))+C_{\ref{firstmomeasbnd}}(\log N)^{-1/2}(X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})+X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2}).

(7.101)

Proof.

Fix
T>0
, let
t\in[0,T]
and
\Psi
be as in
the Lemma, and let
c\in{\mathbb{R}}
. Define
\Phi\in C_{b}([0,t]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d})
by

\Phi(s,x)=e^{-cs}P^{N}_{t-s}\Psi(x)
, so that

\dot{\Phi}(s,x)=-A_{N}\Phi(s,x)-c\Phi(s,x)\in C_{b}([0,t]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2}).

(7.102)

Let

d^{N}(s,\xi^{N}_{s},\Phi)=d^{N,2}(s,\xi^{N}_{s},\Phi)+d^{N,3}(s,\xi^{N}_{s},\Phi)

and
\Theta^{N}=\Theta^{N}_{2}+\Theta^{N}_{3}
. By (
6.10
) and
(
7.102
) we have

E(X^{N}_{t}(\Phi(t,\cdot)))=X^{N}_{0}(P^{N}_{t}(\Psi))+\int_{0}^{t}E(d^{N}(s,\xi^{N}_{s},\Phi)-cX^{N}_{s}(\Phi))\,ds.

(7.103)

Now choose
c>1\vee\sigma\vee(\sup_{N\geq e^{3}}\Theta^{N})
(recall (
7.66
) and
(
7.76
)). It is then easy to see (and in fact is
shown in the proof of Lemma 3.5 in
[
9
]
–see p. 1232)
that
c>1\vee\sigma
implies

\|\Phi\|\leq 2c\|\Psi\|_{\text{{Lip}}}\leq 2cT,

(7.104)

where the last inequality is by
hypothesis. Now use (
7.100
) and
(
7.104
) to deduce that

E\Bigl(\int_{0}^{t}d^{N}(s,\xi^{N}_{s},\Phi)\,ds\Bigr)\leq\int_{0}^{t}cE(X^{N}_{s}(\Phi))\,ds+C_{T}\|\Psi\|_{\text{{Lip}}}(\log N)^{-1/2}(1+X_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2}),\ \ \forall t\in[0,T].

Finally
use this in (
7.103
), noting that

\Phi(t,x)=e^{-ct}\Psi(x)
, to complete the proof with

c_{\ref{firstmomeasbnd}}=c
.
∎

8
Convergence to super-Brownian motion: Proof of Theorem
6.1

We assume the hypotheses of Theorem
6.1
whose proof is the objective of this section.

8.1
Relative compactness

A collection of stochastic processes
\{Y^{N}:N\geq\alpha\}

with sample paths in
D({\mathbb{R}}_{+},S)
for some Polish space
S

is
C
-relatively compact iff for every sequence

N_{k}\uparrow\infty
in
[\alpha,\infty)
there is a
subsequence
\{N^{\prime}_{k}\}
so that
Y^{N^{\prime}_{k}}
converges weakly
in
D({\mathbb{R}}_{+},S)
to a process with continuous paths. The same
definition applies to a given
sequence
of processes.

Proposition 8.1
.

The set
\{X^{N},N\geq N(\varepsilon_{0})\}
is
C
-relatively compact in
D({\mathbb{R}}^{+},{\mathcal{M}_{F}}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}))
.

To prove this it clearly suffices to show that for every

N_{k}\uparrow\infty

(N_{k}\geq N(\varepsilon_{0}))
,
\{X^{N_{k}}\}
, is

C
-relatively compact. To ease the notation we take

N_{k}=k
as the proof in the general case is the same. Hence
we reduce to the case of showing
\{X^{N}:N\in{\mathbb{N}},N\geq N(\varepsilon_{0})\}
is relatively compact. This result will follow
from Jakubowski’s theorem (see Theorem II.4.1 in [P2002])
and the following two lemmas.

Lemma 8.2
.

For any
\Phi\in C_{b}^{3}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
, the sequence

\{X^{N}(\Phi),N\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\geq N(\varepsilon_{0})}\}
is
C
-relatively compact in
D({\mathbb{R}}_{+},{\mathbb{R}})
.

Lemma 8.3
.

For any
\varepsilon>0
,
T>0
there
exists
A>0
such that

\sup_{N\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\geq N(\varepsilon_{0})}}P\left(\sup_{t\leq T}X^{N}_{t}(B(0,A)^{c})>\varepsilon\right)<\varepsilon.

Proof of Lemma
8.2
.
We use
(
6.10
) to establish
C
-relative compactness of

\{X^{N}(\Phi):N\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\geq N(\varepsilon_{0})}\}
by establishing the

C
-relative compactness of each of the terms on the
right-hand side. For the latter we proceed as in Lemma 6.1
of
[
9
]
. Consider first
\{D^{N,j}(\Phi):N\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\geq N(\varepsilon_{0})}\}
for
j=2\text{ or }3
. Use
Proposition
7.6
(b) in (
7.60
) and

\min(a,b)\leq\sqrt{ab}
for
a,b>0
, to see that for

t_{N}\leq s_{1}<s_{2}\leq T
,

\displaystyle E((D^{N,j}_{s_{2}}(\Phi)-D^{N,j}_{s_{1}}(\Phi))^{2})

\displaystyle\leq C_{T}\|\Phi\|_{\text{{Lip}}}^{2}[(s_{2}-s_{1})^{2}+\log^{6}N\sqrt{t_{N}}(s_{2}-s_{1})^{3/2}](X_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})+X_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2})

\displaystyle\leq C_{T}\|\Phi\|_{\text{{Lip}}}^{2}(X_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})+X_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2})(s_{2}-s_{1})^{3/2}.

(8.1)

Moreover, we have from (
7.39
) that for
j=2,3
,

|d^{N,j}(s,\xi_{s}^{N},\Phi)|\leq|\bar{\mathcal{N}}|(\log N)^{3}\|\Phi\|_{\infty}X_{s}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1}),

(8.2)

and so by Proposition
7.6
(b) for
0\leq s_{1}<s_{2}\leq t_{N}
,

\displaystyle E\Bigl(\Bigl(\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}|d^{N,j}(s,\xi^{N}_{s},\Phi)|\,ds\Bigr)^{2}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq C|\bar{\mathcal{N}}|\|\Phi\|_{\infty}^{2}(X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})+X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2})(\log N)^{6}(s_{2}-s_{1})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\Phi\|_{\infty}^{2}(X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})+X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2})(\log N)^{6}\sqrt{t_{N}}(s_{2}-s_{1})^{3/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\Phi\|_{\infty}^{2}(X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})+X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2})(s_{2}-s_{1})^{3/2}.

(8.3)

The
C
-relative compactness of

\{D^{N,j}(\Phi):N\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\geq N(\varepsilon_{0})}\}
is now immediate
from
D_{0}^{N,j}=0
, (
8.1
), (
8.1
) and
Kolmogorov’s criterion.

Turning to the
C
-relative compactness of

\{M^{N}(\Phi):N\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\geq N(\varepsilon_{0})}\}
, as in Lemma 6.1 of

[
9
]
, because the maximum jump of
M^{N}(\Phi)
goes to
zero as
N\to\infty
, it suffices to prove
C
-relative
compactness of
\{\langle M^{N}(\Phi)\rangle:N\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\geq N(\varepsilon_{0})}\}
. From (
6.11
), (
7.50
),
(
7.51
), and the second moment bound in
Proposition
7.6
(b), we have for
0\leq s_{1}<s_{2}\leq T
,

\displaystyle E\Bigl(\Bigl(\langle M^{N}(\Phi)\rangle_{s_{2}}-\langle M^{N}(\Phi)\rangle_{s_{1}}\Bigr)^{2}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq C_{T}\|\Phi\|_{\text{{Lip}}}^{4}(X_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})+X_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2})(s_{2}-s_{1})^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\Phi\|_{\infty}^{4}E\Bigl(\Bigl(\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}Z^{N}_{s}\,ds\Bigr)^{2}\Bigr),

(8.4)

where

Z^{N}_{s}=X^{N}_{s}(\log Nf_{0}^{(N)}(\xi^{N}_{s}))\leq\log NX^{N}_{s}(\mbox{\bf 1}).

(8.5)

Using (
7.54
), the above upper bound, and arguing
exactly as in the proof of (104)–(106) in Lemma 6.1 of

[
9
]
(conditioning back in time by
t_{N}
and employing
the Markov property) we can show that for
T\geq s_{2}>s_{1}\geq t_{N}
,

E\Bigl(\Bigl(\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}Z^{N}_{s}\,ds\Bigr)^{2}\Bigr)\leq C_{T}(X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})+X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2})(s_{2}-s_{1})^{3/2}.

(8.6)

For

0\leq s_{1}<s_{2}\leq t_{N}
we may argue as in (
8.1
)
using (
7.51
), (
7.50
), and the upper bound
in (
8.5
), to see that

E\Bigl(\Bigl(\langle M^{N}(\Phi)\rangle_{s_{2}}-\langle M^{N}(\Phi)\rangle_{s_{1}}\Bigr)^{2}\Bigr)\leq C_{T}\|\Phi\|_{\text{{Lip}}}^{4}(X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})+X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2})(s_{2}-s_{1})^{3/2}.

Combine the above with (
8.1
), and (
8.6
)
to conclude that

E\Bigl(\Bigl(\langle M^{N}(\Phi)\rangle_{s_{2}}-\langle M^{N}(\Phi)\rangle_{s_{1}}\Bigr)^{2}\Bigr)\leq C_{T}\|\Phi\|_{\text{{Lip}}}^{4}(X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})+X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2})(s_{2}-s_{1})^{3/2}\text{
for }0\leq s_{1}<s_{2}\leq T.

(8.7)

The
C
-relative compactness of

\{\langle M^{N}(\Phi)\rangle:N\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\geq N(\varepsilon_{0})}\}
now
follows from Kolmogorov’s criterion.

Finally, the simple argument in Lemma 6.1 of
[
9
]

using Proposition
7.6
(b) shows for
0\leq s_{1}<s_{2}\leq T
,

E\Bigl(\Bigl(D^{N,1}_{s_{2}}(\Phi)-D^{N,1}_{s_{1}}(\Phi)\Bigr)^{2}\Bigr)\leq C_{T,\Phi}(X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})+X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2})(s_{2}-s_{1})^{2}.

(8.8)

(This is one place where the assumed
regularity of
\Phi
is used.) The
C
-relative compactness
of
\{D^{N,1}(\Phi):N\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\geq N(\varepsilon_{0})}\}
follows as
usual. As
X_{0}^{N}(\Phi)\to X_{0}(\Phi)
by hypothesis, the
above results give the
C
-relative compactness of

\{X^{N}(\Phi)\}
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
8.3
.
Let

\{h_{n}:n\in{\mathbb{N}}\}
be a sequence of
[0,1]
-valued functions
in
C^{3}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
such that

\mbox{\bf 1}_{\{|x|>n+1\}}\leq h_{n}(x)\leq\mbox{\bf 1}_{\{|x|>n\}},\ \text{and}\ \sup_{n}\|h_{n}\|_{\text{{Lip}}}\leq C.

(8.9)

For example, if
h:{\mathbb{R}}\to[0,1]
is
C^{\infty}
,
increasing, and
1_{\{z>1\}}\leq h(z)\leq 1_{\{z>0\}}
we can
take
h_{n}(x)=h(|x|-n)
. It clearly suffices to show that for
each fixed
\varepsilon,T>0
,

\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{N\geq N(\varepsilon_{0})}P\Bigl(\sup_{t\leq T}X_{t}^{N}(h_{n})\geq\varepsilon\Bigr)=0.

(8.10)

By (
6.10
)

X^{N}_{t}(h_{n})=M^{N}_{t}(h_{n})+Y^{N}_{t}(h_{n}),

(8.11)

where

Y^{N}_{t}(h_{n})=X_{0}^{N}(h_{n})+\int_{0}^{t}X_{s}^{N}(A_{N}h_{n})\,ds+D^{N,2}_{t}(h_{n})+D^{N,3}_{t}(h_{n}).

Now argue as in the derivation of (110) in the proof of
Lemma 6.1 in
[
9
]
, using (
6.11
),
(
7.50
), (
7.51
), the second inequality in
(
8.9
), and (
7.54
) to conclude that for
some
\varepsilon_{N}\to 0
, independent of
n
,

E(\langle M^{N}(h_{n})\rangle_{T})\leq\varepsilon_{N}+\int_{0}^{T}C_{T}E(X^{N}_{s}(h_{n}^{2}))\,ds\leq\varepsilon_{N}+\int_{0}^{T}C_{T}X_{0}^{N}(P^{N}_{s}(h_{n}^{2}))\,ds.

(8.12)

In the last inequality we used
Lemma
7.19
and absorbed some of the constants and
terms there into the
C_{T}
and
\varepsilon_{N}
. Chebychev’s
inequality (recall (
8.9
)) shows that for any

K>0
,

\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{N\geq N(\varepsilon_{0})}\sup_{|x|\leq K,s\leq T}P_{s}^{N}(h_{n})(x)=0.

The tightness of
\{X_{0}^{N}\}
now shows

\lim_{n\to 0}\sup_{N\geq N(\varepsilon_{0})}\sup_{s\leq T}X^{N}_{0}(P^{N}_{s}(h_{n}))=0,

(8.13)

and so the integral on the righthand side of
(
8.12
) approaches
0
as
n\to\infty
, uniformly
in
N
. For each
N
fixed it is elementary to use
(
6.12
) to see that
\lim_{n}E(\langle M^{N}(h_{n})\rangle_{T})=0
. It now follows from Doob’s strong
inequality
L^{2}
and the above that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{N\geq N(\varepsilon_{0})}E(\sup_{t\leq T}M_{t}^{N}(h_{n})^{2})=0.

(8.14)

To prove (
8.10
), by (
8.11
) it now clearly
suffices to show

\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{N\geq N(\varepsilon_{0})}P(\sup_{t\leq T}|Y^{N}_{t}(h_{n})|\geq\varepsilon)=0.

(8.15)

This is (115) in the proof of Lemma 6.1 in

[
9
]
for the Lotka-Volterra model, and the proof given
there now goes through without change in our more general
setting. The required inputs are (
8.1
),
(
8.1
), (
8.8
), (
8.14
),
Lemma
7.19
, and (
8.13
).
∎

8.2
Identification of the limit

Proof of Theorem
1.11
.
By the
C
-relative
compactness, established above, it remains only to show that
the sequential limit points of
\{X^{N}\}
coincide. By the
Skorokhod representation theorem we may assume that for a
sequence
N_{k}\uparrow\infty,\ N_{k}\geq N(\varepsilon_{0})
, we have

X^{N_{k}}\rightarrow X\in C(R_{+},M_{F}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}))\quad\text{a.s.}

We will take limits in
(
6.10
) and (
6.11
) to see that the law of
X

satisfies (MP), the martingale problem characterizing the
law of SBM
(X_{0},,4\pi\sigma^{2},\sigma^{2},\Theta)
. To see
this, fix
\Phi\in C_{b}^{3}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
. By Lemma 2.6 of

[
6
]
we have

\|A_{N}\Phi-(\sigma^{2}/2)\Delta\Phi\|_{\infty}\to 0\hbox{ as
}N\to\infty,

and therefore by
Proposition
7.6
(a) for all
t>0
,

\lim_{N\to\infty}E\Bigl(\Bigl|\int_{0}^{t}X^{N}_{s}(A_{N}\Phi)\,ds-\int_{0}^{t}X^{N}_{s}((\sigma^{2}/2)\Delta\Phi)\,ds\Bigr|\Bigr)=0.

(8.16)

One now can use Propositions
7.16
and

7.18
, and (
8.16
) to argue exactly as in
the proof of Proposition 3.2 of
[
10
]
and take limits
along
\{N_{k}\}
in (
6.10
), (
6.12
) to see that

X
satisfies (MP) and so is
SBM
(X_{0},,4\pi\sigma^{2},\sigma^{2},\Theta)
. (Only the last two
paragraphs of the proof there are used.) The other inputs
needed there are the
C
-relative compactness of

\{X^{N}(\Phi)\}
,
\{M^{N}(\Phi)\}
, and
\{D^{N,j}(\Phi)\}

for
j=1,2,3
, established in the proof of
Lemma
8.2
, as well as (take
s_{1}=0,s_{2}=T
in
(
8.7
))

E((\langle M^{N}(\Phi)\rangle_{T})^{2})\leq C(T,\Phi)\ \ \text{for all }N.

The latter allows one to conclude that
the limiting
M(\phi)
is a martingale with the appropriate
square function. ∎

Proof of Proposition
7.14

We need an elementary
bound for
p_{t}^{N}(x):=NP(B^{N,0}_{t}=x)
:

p^{N}_{t}(x)\leq\frac{C_{\ref{pNtbound}}}{t}\text{ for all $t>0$, $x\in{S_{N}}$, $N\geq 3$}.

(9.1)

For example, see (A7) in
[
6
]
.

Assume
\delta_{N}>0
converges to zero, and also satisfies

\liminf_{N}\sqrt{N}\delta_{N}>0.

(9.2)

Fix
T\geq 1
and consider
0\leq t\leq T
. Let

p^{N,z}_{s}(x)=p^{N}_{s}(z-x)
and

\phi^{z}_{s}=p^{N,z}_{t-s+\delta_{N}}
. Argue as in the proof of
Proposition 3.10 in Section 5.2 of
[
9
]
, using the
semimartingale decomposition (
6.10
) to see that for a
universal constant
C_{\ref{3.8bound1}}
we have

E\Bigl(\int\int 1_{\{|x-y|\leq\sqrt{\delta_{N}}\}}dX^{N}_{t}(x)dX^{N}_{t}(y)\Bigr)\leq C_{\ref{3.8bound1}}[{\mathcal{T}}_{0}+{\mathcal{T}}_{1}+{\mathcal{T}}_{2}+{\mathcal{T}}_{3}],

(9.3)

where

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta_{N}}{N}\sum_{z\in{S_{N}}}X_{0}^{N}(p^{N,z}_{t+\delta_{N}})^{2}=\int\int\delta_{N}p^{N}_{2(t+\delta_{N})}(y-x)dX^{N}_{0}(x)dX^{N}_{0}(y),

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta_{N}}{N}\sum_{z\in{S_{N}}}E(\langle M(\phi^{z})\rangle_{1,t}),

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta_{N}}{N}\sum_{z\in{S_{N}}}E(\langle M(\phi^{z})\rangle_{2,t}),

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}_{3}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=2}^{3}\frac{\delta_{N}}{N}\sum_{z\in{S_{N}}}E\Bigl(\Bigl(\int_{0}^{t}d^{N,j}(s,\xi^{N}_{s},\phi^{z})\,ds\Bigr)^{2}\Bigr):=\sum_{j=2}^{3}{\mathcal{T}}^{(j)}_{3}.

By (
9.1
),

{\mathcal{T}}_{0}\leq\frac{C_{\ref{e:T0}}\delta_{N}}{t+\delta_{N}}\,X_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2}.

(9.4)

For
{\mathcal{T}}_{2}
, use (
6.12
),

|c^{N,a}(x,\xi^{N}_{s})|=|d^{N,2}(x,\xi^{N}_{s})|
,

|c^{N,s}(x,\xi^{N}_{s})|=|d^{N,3}(x,\xi^{N}_{s})|
, and then
Chapman-Kolmogorov and (
9.1
), to conclude that

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\delta_{N}}{N}E\Bigl(\int_{0}^{t}\frac{(\log N)^{3}}{(N^{\prime})^{2}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}\sum_{z\in{S_{N}}}p^{N}_{t-s+\delta_{N}}(z-x)^{2}\Bigl[|d^{N,2}(x,\xi^{N}_{s})|+|d^{N,3}(x,\xi^{N}_{s})|\Bigr]\,ds\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\delta_{N}}{N}(\log N)^{3}E\Bigl(\int_{0}^{t}\frac{N}{(N^{\prime})^{2}}(t-s+\delta_{N})^{-1}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}[|d^{N,2}(x,\xi^{N}_{s})|+|d^{N,3}(x,\xi^{N}_{s})|]\,ds\Bigr).

(9.5)

From the bounds in (
7.2
) we have

\frac{1}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}|d^{N,j}(x.\xi^{N}_{s})|\leq\frac{\|r\|}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}\sum_{e\in\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}\xi_{s}^{N}(x+e)=\|r\||\bar{\mathcal{N}}|X^{N}_{s}(\mbox{\bf 1}).

Use the above in (
9
) and conclude from
Proposition
7.6
(a) that

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}_{2}\leq\frac{\delta_{N}(\log N)^{4}}{N}E\Bigl(\int_{0}^{t}\frac{C}{t-s+\delta_{N}}X^{N}_{s}(\mbox{\bf 1})\,ds\Bigr)\leq C_{T}\delta_{N}X_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})\log\Bigl(1+\frac{t}{\delta_{N}}\Bigr)\frac{(\log N)^{4}}{N}.

(9.6)

Turning to
{\mathcal{T}}_{1}
, from (
6.12
) and
Chapman-Kolmogorov, we have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}_{1}

\displaystyle=\delta_{N}\log NE\Bigl(\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{N^{\prime}}p^{N}_{2(t-s)+2\delta_{N}}(0)\Bigr[\sum_{x,y\in{S_{N}}}p_{N}(y-x)(\xi^{N}_{s}(x)\widehat{\xi}_{s}^{N}(y)+\widehat{\xi}_{s}^{N}(x)\xi^{N}_{s}(y))\Bigl]\,ds\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\ref{e:T1bnd}}\delta_{N}\log N\int_{0}^{t}(t-s+\delta_{N})^{-1}E\Bigl(\frac{1}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{e\in{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}p_{N}(e)\xi_{s}^{N}(x)\widehat{\xi}^{N}_{s}(x+e)\Bigr)\,ds,

(9.7)

the last by (
9.1
). Set

u_{N}=\frac{\delta_{N}}{2}\wedge(\log N)^{-p}
for some
p\geq 11
. Note that (
9.2
) shows that
u_{N}

satisfies (
7.12
). If
\xi^{N}\in\{0,1\}^{S_{N}}
, define

G^{N}(\xi^{N})=\frac{1}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{e\in{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}p_{N}(e)\xi^{N}(x)\widehat{\xi}^{N}(x+e),

and for
s\geq u_{N}
, let

\displaystyle\Delta_{N}(s)=\Bigl|E(G^{N}(\xi^{N}_{s})|{\mathcal{F}}^{N}_{s-u_{N}})-\widehat{E}\Bigl(\frac{1}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{e\in{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}p_{N}(e)\xi_{s-u_{N}}^{N}(B^{N,x}_{u_{N}})\hat{\xi}^{N}_{s-u_{n}}(B^{N,x+e}_{u_{N}})\Bigr)\Bigr|.

(9.8)

The Markov property and coalescing duality for the voter
model show that

\Delta_{N}(s)=\Bigl|E_{\xi^{N}_{s-u_{N}}}(G^{N}(\xi^{N}_{u_{N}}))-E_{\xi^{N}_{s-u_{N}}}(G^{N}(\xi^{N,{\texttt{vm}}}_{u_{N}}))\Bigr|.

(9.9)

We assume that
\xi^{N}
,
\overline{\xi}^{N}
(the biased voter
model with rates as in (
7.6
)), and

\xi^{N,{\texttt{vm}}}
are constructed as in (
6.2
) and
(
7.11
), all starting at
\xi^{0}_{N}
(with finitely
many ones as usual). Now use the elementary inequality

\displaystyle|\prod_{i=1}^{m}\xi^{N}(x_{i})\prod_{i=m+1}^{m+k}\widehat{\xi}^{N}(x_{i})-\prod_{i=1}^{m}\eta^{N}(x_{i})\prod_{i=m+1}^{m+k}

\displaystyle\widehat{\eta}^{N}(x_{i})|\leq\sum_{i=1}^{m+k}|\xi^{N}(x_{i})-\eta^{N}(x_{i})|

(9.10)

\displaystyle\ \ \forall\,\xi^{N},\eta^{N}\in\{0,1\}^{S_{N}},\ x_{i}\in{S_{N}},

with
m=k=1
, and the coupling

\xi^{N,{\texttt{vm}}}_{u_{N}}\vee\xi^{N}_{u_{N}}\leq\overline{\xi}^{N}_{u_{N}}
,
to conclude that for all
s\geq u_{N}
,

\displaystyle\Delta_{N}(s)

\displaystyle\leq E_{\xi^{N}_{s-u_{N}}}\Bigl(|G^{N}(\xi^{N}_{u_{N}})-G^{N}(\xi^{N,{\texttt{vm}}}_{u_{N}})|\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq E_{\xi^{N}_{\xi^{N}_{s-u_{N}}}}\Bigl(\frac{1}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{e\in{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}p_{N}(e)\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}[(\overline{\xi}^{N}_{u_{N}}(x)-\xi^{N}_{u_{N}}(x))+(\overline{\xi}^{N}_{u_{N}}(x+e)-\xi^{N}_{u_{N}}(x+e))]

\displaystyle\phantom{\leq E_{\xi_{0}^{N}}\Bigl(\frac{1}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{e\in{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}p_{N}(e)\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}}+[(\overline{\xi}^{N}_{u_{N}}(x)-\xi^{N,{\texttt{vm}}}_{u_{N}}(x))+(\overline{\xi}^{N}_{u_{N}}(x+e)-\xi^{N,{\texttt{vm}}}_{u_{N}}(x+e))]\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq 2E_{\xi^{N}_{s-u_{N}}}(2\overline{X}^{N}_{u_{N}}(\mbox{\bf 1})-X_{u_{N}}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})-X_{u_{N}}^{N,{\texttt{vm}}}(\mbox{\bf 1}))\leq C(\log N)^{3-p}X^{N}_{s-u_{N}}(\mbox{\bf 1}).

(9.11)

The last inequality holds by Lemma
7.1
.
For small
s
in (
9
) we will use the crude bound

\frac{1}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{e\in{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}p_{N}(e)\xi_{s}^{N}(x)\widehat{\xi}^{N}_{s}(x+e)\leq X^{N}_{s}(\mbox{\bf 1}).

(9.12)

Use (
9.8
), (
9
) and (
9.12
) (the latter for
s\leq u_{N}
) in (
9
) to see that

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}_{1}\leq

\displaystyle C\delta_{N}\log N\int_{0}^{u_{N}\wedge t}(t-s+\delta_{N})^{-1}E(X_{s}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1}))\,ds+C\delta_{N}\log N\int_{u_{N}}^{u_{N}\vee t}(t-s+\delta_{N})^{-1}E(\Delta_{N}(s))\,ds

\displaystyle+C\delta_{N}\log N\int_{u_{N}}^{u_{N}\vee t}(t-s+\delta_{N})^{-1}E\Bigl(\widehat{E}\Bigl(\frac{1}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{e\in{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}p_{N}(e)\xi_{s-u_{N}}^{N}(B^{N,x}_{u_{N}})\widehat{\xi}^{N}_{s-u_{n}}(B^{N,x+e}_{u_{N}})\Bigr)\Bigr)\,ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{T}\delta_{N}\log NX^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})\int_{0}^{u_{N}\wedge t}(t-s+\delta_{N})^{-1}\,ds+C\delta_{N}(\log N)^{4-p}X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})\int_{u_{N}}^{u_{N}\vee t}(t-s+\delta_{N})^{-1}\,ds

\displaystyle+C\delta_{N}\log N\int_{u_{N}}^{u_{N}\vee t}(t-s+\delta_{N})^{-1}E\Bigl(\widehat{E}\Bigl(\frac{1}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{e\in{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}p_{N}(e)\xi_{s-u_{N}}^{N}(B^{N,x}_{u_{N}})1(\sigma^{N}(x,x+e)>u_{N})\Bigr)\Bigr)\,ds

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}_{1,1}+{\mathcal{T}}_{1,2}+{\mathcal{T}}_{1,3},

(9.13)

where the mean mass bound from Proposition
7.6

(a) is again used in the second inequality. For
s\leq u_{N}(\leq\delta_{N}/2)
we have
s\leq\delta_{N}/2\leq(t+\delta_{N})/2
and so

{\mathcal{T}}_{1,1}\leq C_{T}\delta_{N}\log NX_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})u_{N}2(t+\delta_{N})^{-1}\leq C_{T}X_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})\frac{\delta_{N}}{t+\delta_{N}}.

(9.14)

We also have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}_{1,2}\leq C\delta_{N}(\log N)^{4-p}X_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})1(t>u_{N})\log\Bigl(1+\frac{t}{\delta_{N}}\Bigr).

(9.15)

Next use translation invariance of the coalescing walks,
Proposition
7.6
(a), and (
1.7
) to obtain

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}_{1,3}

\displaystyle\leq C\delta_{N}\log N\int_{u_{N}}^{u_{N}\vee t}(t-s+\delta_{N})^{-1}E\Bigl(\frac{1}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{w\in{S_{N}}}\xi^{N}_{s-u_{N}}(w)

\displaystyle\phantom{\leq C\delta_{N}\log N\int_{u_{N}}^{u_{N}\vee t}(t-s+\delta_{N})}\times\sum_{e\in{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}p_{N}(e)\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}\widehat{P}(B_{u_{N}}^{N,0}=w-x,\sigma^{N}(0,e)>u_{N})\Bigr)\,ds

\displaystyle\leq C\delta_{N}\log N\Bigl[\sum_{e\in{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}p_{N}(e)\widehat{P}(\sigma^{N}(0,e)>u_{N})\Bigr]\int_{u_{N}}^{t\vee u_{N}}(t-s+\delta_{N})^{-1}E(X^{N}_{s-u_{N}}(\mbox{\bf 1}))\,ds

\displaystyle\leq C_{T}\delta_{N}X_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})\frac{\log N}{\log(Nu_{N})}1(t>u_{N})\log\Bigl(\frac{t-u_{N}+\delta_{N}}{\delta_{N}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq C_{T}\delta_{N}X_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})\log\Bigl(1+\frac{t}{\delta_{N}}\Bigr).

(9.16)

In the last line we have used
\delta_{N}\geq cN^{-1/2}
for

N
large (which we may assume), and hence the same for

u_{N}
. Use (
9.14
)-(
9.16
) in
(
9
) to conclude

{\mathcal{T}}_{1}\leq C_{T}X_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})\Bigl[\delta_{N}\log\Bigl(1+\frac{t}{\delta_{N}}\Bigr)+\frac{\delta_{N}}{t+\delta_{N}}\Bigr].

(9.17)

We next decompose
{\mathcal{T}}^{(j)}_{3}
as

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}^{(j)}_{3}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{3\delta_{N}}{N}\sum_{z\in{S_{N}}}E\Bigl(\Bigl(\int_{u_{N}}^{t\vee u_{N}}d^{N,j}(s,\xi^{N}_{s},\phi^{z})-E(d^{N,j}(s,\xi^{N}_{s},\phi^{z})|{\mathcal{F}}^{N}_{s-u_{N}})\,ds\Bigr)^{2}\Bigr)

\displaystyle+\frac{3\delta_{N}}{N}\sum_{z\in{S_{N}}}E\Bigl(\Bigl(\int_{u_{N}}^{t\vee u_{N}}E(d^{N,j}(s,\xi^{N}_{s},\phi^{z})|{\mathcal{F}}^{N}_{s-u_{N}})\,ds\Bigr)^{2}\Bigr)

\displaystyle+\frac{6\delta_{N}}{N}\sum_{z\in{S_{N}}}E\Bigl(\int_{0}^{u_{N}\wedge t}\Bigl[\int_{0}^{s_{1}}d^{N,j}(s_{1},\xi^{N}_{s_{1}},\phi^{z})d^{N,j}(s_{2},\xi^{N}_{s_{2}},\phi^{z})\,ds_{2}\Bigr]\,ds_{1}\Bigr)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}^{(j)}_{3,1}+{\mathcal{T}}^{(j)}_{3,2}+{\mathcal{T}}^{(j)}_{3,3}.

(9.18)

Equation (
7.2
) implies,

\frac{1}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}|d^{N,j}(x,\xi^{N}_{s})|\leq\frac{C}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}\Bigl[\Bigl(\sum_{e\in\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}\xi^{N}_{s}(x+e)\Bigr)\Bigr]\leq C_{\ref{dN*bound}}X_{s}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1}).

(9.19)

Recall that

d^{N,j}(s,\xi^{N}_{s},\phi^{z})=\frac{\ell_{N}^{(j)}}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}p^{N}_{t-s+\delta_{N}}(z-x)d^{N,j}(x,\xi^{N}_{s}).

(9.20)

For
{\mathcal{T}}^{(j)}_{3,3}
, use (
9.20
) (take absolute
values and use the triangle inequality), do the sum over
z

first, and then apply Chapman-Kolmogorov and
(
9.1
) to conclude that

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}^{(j)}_{3,3}\leq\frac{6\delta_{N}}{N}\int_{0}^{u_{N}\wedge t}\int_{0}^{s_{1}}

\displaystyle\frac{(\log N)^{6}}{(N^{\prime})^{2}}NC_{\ref{pNtbound}}(2(t+\delta_{N})-s_{1}-s_{2})^{-1}

(9.21)

\displaystyle\times\sum_{x_{1}\in{S_{N}}}\sum_{x_{2}\in{S_{N}}}E(|d^{N,j}(x_{1},\xi^{N}_{s_{1}})|\,|d^{N,j}(x_{2},\xi^{N}_{s_{2}})|)\,ds_{2}ds_{1}.

An application of (
9.19
) and the
second moment bound (
7.42
) now gives

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}^{(j)}_{3,3}

\displaystyle\leq C\delta_{N}(\log N)^{6}(X_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})+X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2})\int_{0}^{u_{n}}\int_{0}^{s_{1}}(2(t+\delta_{N})-s_{1}-s_{2})^{-1}\,ds_{2}ds_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C\delta_{N}(\log N)^{6-p}(X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})+X_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2}),

(9.22)

the last by a bit of calculus.

For
{\mathcal{T}}^{(j)}_{3,1}
, first use the orthogonality

E\Bigl(\prod_{i=1}^{2}(d^{N,j}(s_{i},\xi^{N}_{s_{i}},\phi^{z})-E(d^{N,j}(s_{i},\xi^{N}_{s_{i}},\phi^{z})|{\mathcal{F}}^{N}_{s_{i}-u_{N}}))\Bigr)=0\text{
if }s_{2}-u_{N}>s_{1}

to see that

{\mathcal{T}}^{(j)}_{3,1}\leq\frac{6\delta_{N}}{N}\sum_{z\in{S_{N}}}E\Bigl(\int_{u_{N}}^{u_{N}\vee t}\Bigl[\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{1}+u_{N}}\prod_{i=1}^{2}\Bigl(|d^{N,j}(s_{i},\xi^{N}_{s_{i}},\phi^{z})|+E\Bigl(|d^{N,j}(s_{i},\xi^{N}_{s_{i}},\phi^{z})|\Bigr|{\mathcal{F}}^{N}_{s_{i}-u_{N}}\Bigr)\Bigr)\,ds_{2}\Bigr]ds_{1}\Bigr).

Argue just as in (
9.21
), but now with a different
product inside the integral, to conclude that

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}^{(j)}_{3,1}\leq

\displaystyle C\delta_{N}(\log N)^{6}\int_{u_{N}}^{u_{N}\vee t}\Bigl[\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{1}+u_{N}}\frac{1}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x_{1}\in{S_{N}}}\frac{1}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x_{2}\in{S_{N}}}(2(t+\delta_{N})-s_{1}-s_{2})^{-1}

\displaystyle\phantom{\frac{C\delta_{N}(\log N)^{6}}{N}\int_{u_{N}}^{u_{N}\vee t}\Bigl[\int}\times E\Bigl(\prod_{i=1}^{2}\Bigl[|d^{N,j}(x_{i},\xi^{N}_{s_{i}})|+E(|d^{N,j}(x_{i},\xi^{N}_{s_{i}})||{\mathcal{F}}^{N}_{s_{i}-u_{N}})\Bigr]\Bigr)ds_{2}ds_{1}.

Now bring the sums through the expectation and product and
apply (
9.19
) to conclude

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}^{(j)}_{3,1}\leq

\displaystyle C\delta_{N}(\log N)^{6}\int_{u_{N}}^{u_{N}\vee t}\Bigl[\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{1}+u_{N}}(2(t+\delta_{N})-s_{1}-s_{2})^{-1}E\Bigl(\Bigl(X^{N}_{s_{1}}(\mbox{\bf 1})+E(X^{N}_{s_{1}}(\mbox{\bf 1})|{\mathcal{F}}^{N}_{s_{1}-u_{N}})\Bigr)

\displaystyle\phantom{C\delta_{N}(\log N)^{6}\int_{u_{N}}^{u_{N}\vee t}(2(t+\delta_{N})-s_{1}-s_{2})}\times\Bigl(X^{N}_{s_{2}}(\mbox{\bf 1})+E(X^{N}_{s_{2}}(\mbox{\bf 1})|{\mathcal{F}}^{N}_{s_{2}-u_{N}})\Bigr)\Bigr)\,ds_{2}\Bigr]ds_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\delta_{N}(\log N)^{6}\int_{u_{N}}^{u_{N}\vee t}\Bigl[\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{1}+u_{N}}(2(t+\delta_{N})-s_{1}-s_{2})^{-1}

\displaystyle\phantom{C\delta_{N}(\log N)^{6}\int_{u_{N}}^{u_{N}\vee t}}\times E((X^{N}_{s_{1}}(\mbox{\bf 1})+X^{N}_{s_{1}-u_{N}}(\mbox{\bf 1}))(X^{N}_{s_{2}}(\mbox{\bf 1})+X^{N}_{s_{2}-u_{N}}(\mbox{\bf 1})))\,ds_{2}\Bigr]ds_{1},

where in the last we have used the Markov property and mean
mass bound from Propostion

. The second moment
bound (
7.42
) from the same result therefore shows
that

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}^{(j)}_{3,1}\leq

\displaystyle C_{T}\delta_{N}(X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})+X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2})(\log N)^{6}u_{N}\int_{u_{N}}^{u_{N}\vee t}\frac{1}{2(t-s_{1}+\delta_{N})-u_{N}}\,ds_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{T}\delta_{N}(X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})+X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2})(\log N)^{6-p}1(t>u_{N})\log\Bigl(\frac{2(t+\delta_{N})-3u_{N}}{2\delta_{N}-u_{N}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{T}\delta_{N}(X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})+X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2})(\log N)^{6-p}\log\Bigl(\frac{2(t+\delta_{N})}{\delta_{N}}\Bigr)\quad\text{ (recall $u_{N}\leq\delta_{N}$)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{T}(X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})+X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2})(\log N)^{6-p}\delta_{N}\Bigr[\log 2+\log\Bigl(1+\frac{t}{\delta_{N}}\Bigr)\Bigl].

(9.23)

Turning to

(9.28)

In the last we used Lemma
7.1
. For
