(9.37)

In the last line we have used (
7.42
).

The corresponding bound on
\hat{\mathcal{T}}_{3,2}^{(2)}
is much
simpler. The analogue of (
9
) is

\sum_{i=2}^{\bar{\mathcal{N}}}\sum_{\emptyset\neq A\subset{\mathcal{N}}}|r^{N,a}(\sqrt{N}A)|\hat{E}(I^{N,+}_{i}(x,u_{N},A,\xi^{N}_{0}))\leq CX_{0}^{N}(1)(\log N)^{-1}.

Now proceed as in (
9
) using the supnorm
bound on
p^{N}_{2(t+\delta_{N}-s_{1}-s_{2}}(x_{1}-x_{2})
to see that

\displaystyle|\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{(2)}_{3,2}|

\displaystyle\leq C\delta_{N}\int_{u_{N}}^{t\vee u_{N}}\int_{u_{N}}^{s_{1}}(2(t+\delta_{N})-s_{1}-s_{2})^{-1}E(X^{N}_{s_{1}-u_{N}}(1)X^{N}_{s_{2}-u_{N}}(1))\,ds_{1}ds_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{T}\delta_{N}(X_{0}^{N}(1)+X_{0}^{N}(1)^{2}).

(9.38)

Finally use the decompositions (
9.3
) and
(
9
) along with the bounds on
{\mathcal{T}}_{0}
,
{\mathcal{T}}_{2}
,

{\mathcal{T}}_{1}
,
{\mathcal{T}}_{3.3}
,
{\mathcal{T}}_{3.1}
,
|{\mathcal{T}}^{j)}_{3.2}-\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{(j)}_{3.2}|
, and
|\hat{T}^{(j)}_{3.2}|
from
(
9.4
), (
9.6
), (
9.17
), (
9
),
(
9
), (
9.29
), (
9
) and
(
9.38
), respectively, to see that

E\Bigl(\int\int 1_{\{|x-y|\leq\sqrt{\delta_{N}}\}}dX^{N}_{t}(x)dX^{N}_{t}(y)\Bigr)\leq C_{T}(X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})+X_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2})\delta_{N}\Bigl(1+\log\Bigl(1+\frac{t}{\delta_{N}}\Bigr)+(t+\delta_{N})^{-1}\Bigr).

The result follows by noting that

\log\Bigl(1+\frac{t}{\delta_{N}}\Bigr)+(t+\delta_{N})^{-1}
is
bounded away from
0
uniformly in
N
,
t>0
and so we can
drop the initial
1
on the right-hand side. ∎

10
Proof of Theorem
4.9

We assume the hypotheses of Theorem
4.9
, and
work in the setting of Sections
4
and

6
, so that the monotone,
asymptotically symmetric voter model perturbation,

\{\xi^{[\varepsilon]}:0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}\}
is constructed as in
Proposition (
4.3
)(a), along with it’s
associated measure-valued process
X^{N}
in
(
6.5
). For real numbers
K_{0}>2
and
L^{\prime}>3
, let

I=[-L^{\prime},L^{\prime}]^{2}
,
\tilde{I}=(-K_{0}L^{\prime},K_{0}L^{\prime})^{2}
, and recall the
notation
I_{\pm e_{i}}=\pm L^{\prime}e_{i}+[-L^{\prime}+1,L^{\prime}-1]^{2}
,
i=1,2
,
from (
4
). Recall also that
N
is chosen as in
(
3.16
). We construct our killed processes

\underline{\xi}^{[\varepsilon]}
as in (
4.5
), where killing
is done when
|x|\geq M_{0}:=\lfloor\sqrt{N}K_{0}L^{\prime}\rfloor

(
x\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
). We define

\underline{\xi}^{N}_{t}(x)=\underline{\xi}^{[\varepsilon_{N}]}_{Nt}(\sqrt{N}x)
for
x\in{S_{N}}
as in (
4.16
), and let

\xi^{N}_{t}(x)=\xi^{[\varepsilon_{N}]}_{Nt}(\sqrt{N}x)
for
x\in{S_{N}}
as
in Section
6
. The killed measure
valued process
\underline{X}^{N}
is defined as in
(
4.17
) and so the killing here is done for

|x|\geq K_{0}L^{\prime}
(
x\in{S_{N}}
). By
Proposition
4.3
(a),(b) our processes are
therefore coupled so that

\underline{\xi}^{N}\leq\xi^{N}\text{
and hence }\underline{X}^{N}\leq X^{N}.

(10.1)

Recall from Section
7
that
(P^{N}_{t},t\geq 0)

is the semigroup of a rate
N
random walk on
{S_{N}}
with
step kernel
p_{N}
. For
x\in{S_{N}}
let
\tilde{B}^{N,x}

denote a random walk starting at
x\in{S_{N}}
with semigroup

(P^{N}_{t})
.

A key step in verifying condition (
4
) is to show that the killed and unkilled processes are close
on certain time scales through the following version of Lemma 8.1 of
[
9
]
. We stress that for now
K_{0}
and
L^{\prime}
are arbitrary real parameters.

Lemma 10.1
.

There is a positive constant

c_{\ref{l:CMPL81}}
and a nondecreasing
function
C_{\ref{l:CMPL81}}:{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\to{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
so that if
t>0
,

K_{0}>2
and
L^{\prime}>3
, and

\underline{X}^{N}_{0}=X^{N}_{0}

is supported on
I
, then

\displaystyle E[X^{N}_{t}(\mbox{\bf 1})-\underline{X}^{N}_{t}(\mbox{\bf 1})]\leq X_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})\Bigl[c_{\ref{l:CMPL81}}

\displaystyle e^{c_{\ref{l:CMPL81}}t}P\Bigl(\sup_{s\leq t}|\tilde{B}^{N,0}_{s}|>(K_{0}-1)L^{\prime}-3\Bigr)

\displaystyle+C_{\ref{l:CMPL81}}(t)(1\vee X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1}))(\log N)^{-1/6}\Bigr].

(10.2)

The Lemma is proved below but we first turn to the
main result of this Section. Given Lemma
10.1
,
the proof of Theorem
4.9
is done just as the
proof of Lemma 6.2 of
[
13
]
(for Lotka-Volterra
models). We outline the argument below for completeness.

Proof of Theorem
4.9
(sketch).
Recall that we must show, after perhaps reducing
\varepsilon_{0}
, that

\displaystyle\text{There are
$T^{\prime}>1$, $K,J^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{N}}$ with $K>2$, and $L^{\prime}>3$, so that if
}0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0},

\displaystyle\text{then for }L=\lfloor\sqrt{N}L^{\prime}\rfloor,\,\underline{X}^{N}_{0}([-L^{\prime},L^{\prime}]^{2})\geq J^{\prime}\text{ implies }

\displaystyle P(\underline{X}_{T^{\prime}}^{N}(I_{e})\geq J^{\prime}\text{ for all }e\in\{\pm e_{i},i=1,2\})\geq 1-6^{-5(2K+1)^{3}}.

(10.3)

Note first that our hypotheses imply that
Theorem
6.1
holds. The limiting super-Brownian
motion in that result has drift
\Theta_{2}+\Theta_{3}
, which
is positive by hypothesis, and therefore will continue to
grow exponentially up to time
T^{\prime}
with high probability if
it has a large enough initial mass. It is therefore not hard
to prove an analogue of (
10
) for this
limiting process (see (6.7) of
[
13
]
). By the
convergence theorem (Theorem
6.1
), the same bound
will hold for
X^{N}_{T^{\prime}}
if
N
is large enough, and so

\varepsilon
is sufficiently small. This is where we may need to
reduce
\varepsilon_{0}
. Here we also use monotonicity to reduce to
the case where
X^{N}_{0}
is finite and apply a subsequence
argument to assume these initial measures converge and so
the convergence theorem holds. To derive the same bound for

\underline{X}^{N}
, and hence gain the necessary spatial
independence required for our comparison to oriented
percolation, we need to show
\underline{X}_{T^{\prime}}^{N}
is close
to
X_{T^{\prime}}^{N}
. This is where Lemma
10.1
is
needed. The inputs required to carry out the proof of
Lemma 6.2 of
[
13
]
are Lemma
10.1
and the
weak convergence of the rescaled process to SBM with a
positive drift, given here by Theorem
6.1
. Our
definition of
I_{e}
for
e=\pm e_{i}
is slightly different
from that in
[
13
]
but it results in only a trivial
change. Also we have been a bit more careful in choosing
integer parameters here. So once
K\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
L^{\prime}>3
are
chosen as in the proof of Lemma 6.2 of
[
13
]
, one
takes
N
large and sets
L=\lfloor\sqrt{N}L^{\prime}\rfloor
(as in
(
10
)), and then chooses
K_{0}=K_{0}(N)(>2)
so
that
K_{0}L^{\prime}\sqrt{N}=KL
. (In this way
K_{0}
is comparable to

K
.) This equality ensures killing for the unscaled process
outside
(-KL,KL)^{2}
corresponds to killing

\underline{X}^{N}
outside
\tilde{I}
as in
Lemma
10.1
. (Note that
K_{0}
may depend on
N
in
Lemma
10.1
.) The rest of the argument is now
identical to that of Lemma 6.2 in
[
13
]
. ∎

Recall from Section
7
that

\{B^{N,x}:\,x\in{S_{N}}\}
is a system of rate
w_{N}N

coalescing random walks in
{S_{N}}
with step kernel
p_{N}
.
Let
T^{\prime}_{x}=\inf\{t\geq 0:B_{t}^{N,x}\notin\tilde{I}\}
, let

\Delta
denote a cemetery state, and define a “killed”
coalescing random walk system,

\{\underline{B}^{N,x}_{t},x\in{S_{N}}\}
, by

\underline{B}^{N,x}_{t}=\begin{cases}B^{N,x}_{t}&\text{if }t<T^{\prime}_{x}\\
\Delta&\text{if }t\geq T^{\prime}_{x}.\end{cases}

We define the killed random walk
\tilde{\underline{B}}^{N,x}
(recall
the step rate here is
N
and there is no coalescing) in the
same way and denote its associated killed semigroup by

({\underline{P}}^{N}_{t},t\geq 0)
. Of course,

{\underline{B}}^{N,x}_{t}=\tilde{\underline{B}}^{N,x}\equiv\Delta
for all

x\notin\tilde{I}
. We will use the convention that

\xi(\Delta)=0
for all

\xi\in\{0,1\}^{S_{N}}
.

Proof of Lemma
10.1
.
We follow the proof of
Lemma 8.1 in
[
9
]
for
2
-dimensional Lotka-Volterra
models, but some modifications are needed. Assume
X^{N}_{0}

(and hence
\xi^{N}_{0}=\underline{\xi}^{N}_{0}
) is supported on

I=[-L^{\prime},L^{\prime}]^{2}
and
T^{\prime}>0
. Let
f:{S_{N}}\cup\{\Delta\}\to{\mathbb{R}}

with
f(\Delta)=0
and set
\Phi(s,x)={\underline{P}}^{N}_{t-s}f(x)
,

s\leq t
. We will assume
t\in[0,T^{\prime}]
in what follows. The
killed analogue of (
6.10
) is derived as in the proof
of Lemma 3.2 of
[
11
]
where a general class of voter
model perturbations is considered. The argument there uses a
different representation for the perturbation but applies to
our representations without change and gives (see the last
display on p. 113 of
[
11
]
)

\underline{X}^{N}_{t}(f)=\underline{X}^{N}_{0}({\underline{P}}^{N}_{t}f)+\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{j=2}^{3}d^{N,j}(s,\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s},\Phi)ds+\underline{M}^{N}_{t}(\Phi),

(10.4)

where
\underline{M}^{N}_{t}(\Phi)
is a square-integrable, mean
zero martingale. Next, choose
h:{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\cup\{\Delta\}\to[0,1]
such that
h(\Delta)=0
and

[-K_{0}L^{\prime}+3,K_{0}L^{\prime}-3]^{2}\subset\{h=1\}\subset\text{Supp}(h)\subset[-K_{0}L^{\prime}+2,K_{0}L^{\prime}-2]^{2},\quad|h|_{\text{{Lip}}}\leq 1,

and define, for
s\leq t
and
x\in{S_{N}}
,
\Psi(s,x)=\underline{P}^{N}_{t-s}h(x).
By Lemma 8.4 in
[
9
]
there
is a constant
C_{\ref{Psi1/2}}>0
such that

\|\Psi\|_{N}\leq C_{\ref{Psi1/2}}\ \ \text{ for all }N.

(10.5)

By (
7.103
) (with
c=0
and
\Phi=\mbox{\bf 1}
),
(
10.4
) (with
\Phi=\Psi
and
f=h
), the inequality

h\leq 1
and
X^{N}_{0}=\underline{X}^{N}_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle E[X^{N}_{t}(\mbox{\bf 1})-\underline{X}^{N}_{t}(\mbox{\bf 1})]

\displaystyle\leq E[X^{N}_{t}(\mbox{\bf 1})-\underline{X}^{N}_{t}(h)]

\displaystyle=X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1}-{\underline{P}}^{N}_{t}(h))+E\Big[\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{j=2}^{3}(d^{N,j}(s,\xi^{N}_{s},\mbox{\bf 1})-d^{N,j}(s,\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s},\Psi))\,ds\Big].

(10.6)

It follows that

E[X^{N}_{t}(\mbox{\bf 1})-\underline{X}^{N}_{t}(\mbox{\bf 1})]\leq{\mathcal{U}}_{0}+{\mathcal{U}}_{1}+{\mathcal{U}}_{2}+{\mathcal{U}}_{3}+{\mathcal{U}}_{4},

(10.7)

where

\displaystyle{\mathcal{U}}_{0}

\displaystyle=X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1}-{\underline{P}}^{N}_{t}(h)),

\displaystyle{\mathcal{U}}_{1}

\displaystyle=E\Big[\int_{0}^{t_{N}\wedge t}\sum_{j=2}^{3}(d^{N,j}(s,\xi^{N}_{s},\mbox{\bf 1})-d^{N,j}(s,\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s},\Psi))\,ds\Big],

\displaystyle{\mathcal{U}}_{2}

\displaystyle=E\Big[\int_{t_{N}}^{t\vee t_{N}}\sum_{j=2}^{3}d^{N,j}(s,\xi^{N}_{s},\mbox{\bf 1}-\Psi)\,ds\Big],

\displaystyle{\mathcal{U}}_{3}

\displaystyle=E\Big[\int_{t_{N}}^{t\vee t_{N}}(d^{N,2}(s,\xi^{N}_{s},\Psi)-d^{N,2}(s,\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s},\Psi))\,ds\Big],

\displaystyle{\mathcal{U}}_{4}

\displaystyle=E\Big[\int_{t_{N}}^{t\vee t_{N}}(d^{N,3}(s,\xi^{N}_{s},\Psi)-d^{N,3}(s,\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s},\Psi))\,ds\Big].

The labeling matches that of (127) in
[
9
]
.

We claim that there is a positive function

C_{\ref{U1}}:(0,\infty)\to(0,\infty)
and positive
constants
\tilde{K}
,
c_{\ref{U2}}
such that for any

t\leq T^{\prime}
, if
|\cdot|_{\infty}
denotes the
L^{\infty}
norm
on
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
, then

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{U}}_{0}|

\displaystyle\leq X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})P\Big(\sup_{u\leq t}|\tilde{B}^{N,0}_{u}|_{\infty}>(K_{0}-1)L^{\prime}-3\Big),

(10.8)

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{U}}_{1}|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\ref{U1}}(T^{\prime})X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})(\log N)^{3}t_{N},

(10.9)

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{U}}_{2}|

\displaystyle\leq X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})\Big[c_{\ref{U2}}e^{c_{\ref{U2}}t}\hat{P}\Big(\sup_{u\leq t}|{\tilde{B}}^{N,0}_{u}|_{\infty}>(K_{0}-1)L^{\prime}-3\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+C_{\ref{U1}}(T^{\prime})(1\vee X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1}))(\log N)^{-1/2}\Big],

(10.10)

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{U}}_{j}|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\ref{U1}}(T^{\prime})(\log N)^{-1/6}(X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})+X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2})+\tilde{K}\int_{0}^{t}E[X^{N}_{s}(\mbox{\bf 1})-\underline{X}^{N}_{s}(\mbox{\bf 1})]ds,\ j=3,4.

(10.11)

Assuming
(
10.8
)–(
10.11
) for now, and recalling that
t_{N}=(\log N)^{-19}
,
we see that for some function
C:(0,\infty)\to(0,\infty)
, and all
t\leq T^{\prime}
,

E[X^{N}_{t}(\mbox{\bf 1})-\underline{X}_{t}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})]\leq X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})\Big[c_{\ref{U2}}e^{c_{\ref{U2}}t}P\Bigl(\sup_{s\leq t}|{\tilde{B}}^{N,0}_{s}|_{\infty}>(K_{0}-1)L^{\prime}-3\Bigr)\\
+C(T^{\prime})(1\vee X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1}))(\log N)^{-1/6}\Big]+2\tilde{K}\int_{0}^{t}E[X^{N}_{s}(\mbox{\bf 1})-\underline{X}^{N}_{s}(\mbox{\bf 1})]ds.

By replacing
C(T^{\prime})
with
\inf_{T\geq T^{\prime}}C(T)
we may assume
that
C(\cdot)
is non-decreasing. Now take
T^{\prime}=t
in the
above and use Gronwall’s inequality to complete the proof of
Lemma
10.1
.

Thus, our remaining task in this section is to verify
(
10.8
)–(
10.11
). Equation (
10.8
) is (128) of

[
9
]
(which uses the fact that
X_{0}^{N}
is supported on

and so applies here as well). Equation (

)
follows from (
8.2
) and its counterpart for

\underline{\xi}^{N}
(the proof is the same), the mean mass
bound Proposition
7.6
(a), and
\underline{X}^{N}\leq X^{N}
.

The derivation of (
10.10
) follows that of (130) in

[
9
]
. Since it does not involve
\underline{\xi}^{N}_{t}

we can apply the bounds from Section
7
. We
have

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{U}}_{2}|\leq

\displaystyle\Big|E\Bigl(\int_{t_{N}}^{t\vee t_{N}}\sum_{j=2}^{3}\big(E(d^{N,j}(s,\xi^{N}_{s},1-\Psi)|{\mathcal{F}}_{s-t_{N}})-\Theta^{N}_{j}X^{N}_{s-t_{N}}(1-\Psi_{s-t_{N}})\big)\,ds\Bigr)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad+\Big|E\Bigl(\int_{t_{N}}^{t\vee t_{N}}(\Theta^{N}_{2}+\Theta^{N}_{3})X^{N}_{s-t_{N}}(1-\Psi_{s-t_{N}})\,ds\Bigr)\Bigr|

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle|\Delta_{1}|+|\Delta_{2}|.

(10.12)

For
\Delta_{1}
, first use Proposition
7.12

and (
10.5
), and then Corollary
7.15
and
Proposition
7.6
(a) to see that for some

C_{\ref{U2b}}(T^{\prime})
(and all
t\leq T^{\prime}
)

|\Delta_{1}|\leq C_{\ref{U2b}}(T^{\prime})(\log N)^{-1/2}(X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})+X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2}).

(10.13)

For
\Delta_{2}
, use (
7.66
), (
7.76
), and
Lemma
7.19
(recall

\|\Psi_{s-t_{N}}\|_{\text{{Lip}}}\leq C_{\ref{Psi1/2}}

to apply

), and then use

P^{N}_{s-t_{N}}\Psi_{s-t_{N}}\geq\underline{P}^{N}_{s-t_{N}}\Psi_{s-t_{N}}=\underline{P}^{N}_{t}h

to see that for some constants
c_{i}>0
,

\displaystyle|\Delta_{2}|

\displaystyle\leq c_{1}\int_{t_{N}}^{t\vee t_{N}}e^{c_{\ref{firstmomeasbnd}}(t-s)}X_{0}^{N}(P^{N}_{s-t_{N}}(1-\Psi_{s-t_{N}}))\,ds+C_{\ref{firstmomeasbnd}}(T^{\prime})(\log N)^{-1/2}(X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})+X_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2})

\displaystyle\leq c_{1}\int_{t_{N}}^{t\vee t_{N}}e^{c_{\ref{firstmomeasbnd}}(t-s)}X_{0}^{N}(1-\underline{P}^{N}_{t}h)\,ds+C_{\ref{firstmomeasbnd}}(T^{\prime})(\log N)^{-1/2}(X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})+X_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2})

\displaystyle\leq c_{2}e^{c_{\ref{firstmomeasbnd}}t}X_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})P(\sup_{u\leq t}|\tilde{B}^{N,0}_{u}|_{\infty}>(K_{0}-1)L^{\prime}-3)+C_{\ref{firstmomeasbnd}}(T^{\prime})(\log N)^{-1/2}(X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})+X_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2}),

(10.14)

where (
10.8
) is used in the last line. So by
(
10.12
)-(

), we have (
10.10
).

We turn now to the more involved proof of (
10.11
).
Recall from Section
7.1
, that
\xi_{t}^{N,{\texttt{vm}}}

denotes a rescaled voter model on
{S_{N}}
with rate function

Nw_{N}c^{N,{\texttt{vm}}}(x,\xi)
. Let
\underline{\xi}^{N,{\texttt{vm}}}
denote the
corresponding killed voter model, which has rate function

Nw_{N}c^{N,{\texttt{vm}}}(x,\xi)1(x\in\tilde{I})
and initial condition

\underline{\xi}_{0}^{N}
supported on
\tilde{I}
. We will assume

\xi^{N,{\texttt{vm}}}
has the same initial condition and so by the
monotonicity of the voter model, just as in
(
10.1
), we may assume

\underline{\xi}^{N,{\texttt{vm}}}\leq\xi^{N,{\texttt{vm}}}.

(10.15)

We will also use the following killed duality equation which
is a special case of (9.36) in
[
12
]
:

For
\underline{\xi}^{N}_{0}
supported in
\tilde{I}
and
finite disjoint
A,B\subset{S_{N}}
,

\displaystyle E_{\underline{\xi}^{N}_{0}}\Big[\prod_{a\in A}\underline{\xi}^{N,{\texttt{vm}}}_{t}(a)\prod_{b\in B}(1-\underline{\xi}^{N,{\texttt{vm}}}_{t}(b))\Big]=\widehat{E}\Big[\prod_{a\in A}\underline{\xi}^{N}_{0}({\underline{B}}^{N,a}_{t})\prod_{b\in B}(1-\underline{\xi}^{N}_{0}({\underline{B}}^{N,b}_{t}))\Big].

(10.16)

In view of the above we assume for now that
\underline{\xi}^{N}_{0}
is
supported on the larger set
\tilde{I}
. Recalling
(
9.25
), for
u>0
and
j=2,3
we define

\hat{}\underline{H}^{N,j}(\underline{\xi}^{N}_{0},x,u)=E_{\underline{\xi}^{N}_{0}}(d^{N,j}(x,\underline{\xi}_{u}^{N,{\texttt{vm}}})).

(10.17)

By (
10.16
) and just as in the derivation of
(
7.20
),

\displaystyle\hat{}\underline{H}^{N,2}(\underline{\xi}^{N}_{0},x,u)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\emptyset\neq A\subset{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}r^{N,a}(\sqrt{N}A)\widehat{E}\Big(\prod_{y\in A}\underline{\xi}^{N}_{0}({\underline{B}}^{N,x+y}_{u})\prod_{z\in\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}\setminus A}(1-\underline{\xi}^{N}_{0}({\underline{B}}^{N,x+z}_{u}))\Big)

\displaystyle\hat{}\underline{H}^{N,3}(\underline{\xi}^{N}_{0},x,u)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\emptyset\neq A\subset{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}r^{N,s}(\sqrt{N}A)\widehat{E}\Big(\prod_{y\in A}\underline{\xi}^{N}_{0}({\underline{B}}^{N,x+y}_{u})\prod_{z\in\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}\setminus A}(1-\underline{\xi}^{N}_{0}({\underline{B}}^{N,x+z}_{u}))

\displaystyle\phantom{=\sum_{\emptyset\neq A\subsetneq{\mathcal{N}}}r^{\varepsilon_{N}}_{|A|}}-\prod_{y\in A}(1-\underline{\xi}^{N}_{0}({\underline{B}}^{N,x+y}_{u}))\prod_{z\in\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}\setminus A}\underline{\xi}^{N}_{0}({\underline{B}}^{N,x+z}_{u})\Big).

With the definitions (
7.21
) and (
7.22
) in mind
we also introduce

\displaystyle\hat{}\underline{H}^{N,j}(\underline{\xi}^{N}_{0},u,\Psi_{s})

\displaystyle=\frac{\ell^{(j)}_{N}}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}\Psi(s,x)\hat{}\underline{H}^{N,j}(\underline{\xi}^{N}_{0},x,u),\ j=2,3.

As for (
7.23
), (
10.16
) implies for
j=2,3
,

E_{\underline{\xi}^{N}_{0}}[d^{N,j}(s,\underline{\xi}^{N,{\texttt{vm}}}_{u},\Psi)]=\hat{}\underline{H}^{N,j}(\underline{\xi}^{N}_{0},u,\Psi_{s}).

The next result is a killed version of Lemma
7.3
. We
give the proof at the end of this section.

Lemma 10.2
.

There is a constant

C_{\ref{d3H_}}
such that for
j=2,3
, all
T^{\prime}>0
,
\Phi\in C_{b}([0,T^{\prime}]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2})
and all
s\in[t_{N},T^{\prime}]
,

\Big|E_{\underline{\xi}^{N}_{0}}\big[d^{N,j}(s,\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s},\Phi)|{\mathcal{F}}^{N}_{s-t_{N}}\big]-\hat{}\underline{H}^{N,j}(\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s-t_{N}},t_{N},\Phi_{s-t_{N}})\Big|\leq C_{\ref{d3H_}}\|\Phi\|_{1/2,N}(\log N)^{-13/2}\underline{X}^{N}_{s-t_{N}}(\mbox{\bf 1}).

(10.18)

Let us assume again that
\xi^{N}_{0}=\underline{\xi}^{N}_{0}
is supported on
the smaller set
I
(as opposed to
\tilde{I}
), and for

j=2,3
write
{\mathcal{U}}_{j+1}=\sum_{i=1}^{5}{\mathcal{V}}_{j,i}
, where

\displaystyle{\mathcal{V}}_{j,1}

\displaystyle=E\Big[\int_{t_{N}}^{t_{N}\vee t}[d^{N,j}(s,\xi^{N}_{s},\Psi)-\Theta^{N}_{j}X^{N}_{s-t_{N}}(\Psi)]ds\Big],

(10.19)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{V}}_{j,2}

\displaystyle=\Theta^{N}_{j}E\Big[\int_{t_{N}}^{t_{N}\vee t}[X_{s-t_{N}}(\Psi)-\underline{X}_{s-t_{N}}(\Psi)]ds\Big],

(10.20)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{V}}_{j,3}

\displaystyle=E\Big[\int_{t_{N}}^{t_{N}\vee t}[\Theta^{N}_{j}\underline{X}_{s-t_{N}}(\Psi)-\hat{H}^{N,j}(\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s-t_{N}},t_{N},\Psi_{s-t_{N}})]ds\Big],

(10.21)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{V}}_{j,4}

\displaystyle=E\Big[\int_{t_{N}}^{t_{N}\vee t}[\hat{H}^{N,j}(\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s-t_{N}},t_{N},\Psi_{s-t_{N}})-\hat{}\underline{H}^{N,j}(\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s-t_{N}},t_{N},\Psi_{s-t_{N}})]ds\Big],

(10.22)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{V}}_{j,5}

\displaystyle=E\Big[\int_{t_{N}}^{t_{N}\vee t}[\hat{}\underline{H}^{N,j}(\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s-t_{N}},t_{N},\Psi_{s-t_{N}})-d^{N,j}(s,\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s},\Psi)]ds.

(10.23)

We bound these one at a time.

By (
7.91
) and (
7.98
),

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{V}}_{j,1}|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\ref{dsThtX}}\|\Psi\|_{N}\int_{0}^{(t-t_{N})^{+}}\Bigl(\frac{1}{t_{N}\log N}E[\mathscr{I}^{N}(\xi^{N}_{s})]+(\log N)^{-1/2}E[X_{s}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})]\Bigr)ds

(10.24)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\ref{dsThtX}}\|\Psi\|_{N}\Big[C_{\ref{keyint}}(T^{\prime})(\log N)^{-1/2}\Big(X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})+X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2}\Big)+(\log N)^{-1/2}\int_{0}^{(t-t_{N})^{+}}E[X^{N}_{s}(\mbox{\bf 1})]ds\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\ref{U41bnd}}(T^{\prime})(\log N)^{-1/2}\Big(X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})+X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2}\Big),

(10.25)

where we have used
Proposition
7.6
(a) and (
10.5
) in the last
step.
Recalling that
\underline{\xi}^{N}_{t}\leq\xi^{N}_{t}
, letting

\tilde{K}=\sup_{N}(|\Theta^{N}_{2}|+|\Theta^{N}_{3}|)<\infty

and using

\|\Psi\|_{\infty}\leq 1
, we get

|{\mathcal{V}}_{j,2}|\leq\tilde{K}\int_{0}^{(t-t_{N})^{+}}E[X_{s}(\mbox{\bf 1})-\underline{X}_{s}(\mbox{\bf 1})]ds.

(10.26)

Change variables in
{\mathcal{V}}_{j,3}
to rewrite it as an integral over

[0,(t-t_{N})^{+}]
, set
u=s
and replace
\xi^{N}_{0}
with
\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s}

in Proposition
7.10
and Proposition
7.11

to obtain

|{\mathcal{V}}_{j,3}|\leq C(T^{\prime})\|\Psi\|_{N}\int_{0}^{(t-t_{N})^{+}}\Bigl(\frac{1}{t_{N}\log N}E[\mathscr{I}^{N}(\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s})]+(\log N)^{-1/2}E[\underline{X}_{s}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})]\Bigr)ds,

which is the same as the right-hand side of (
10.24
),
but with
\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s}
instead of
\xi^{N}_{s}
. Since
\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s}\leq\xi^{N}_{s}
, (
10.25
) gives

|{\mathcal{V}}_{j,3}|\leq C_{\ref{U41bnd}}(\log N)^{-1/2}\Big(X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})+X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})^{2}\Big).

(10.27)

By (
10.5
), Lemma
10.2
,
\underline{X}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})\leq X^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})
and
Proposition
7.6
(a),

|{\mathcal{V}}_{j,5}|\leq C_{\ref{d3H_}}\|\Psi\|_{1/2,N}(\log N)^{-13/2}\int_{0}^{(t-t_{N})^{+}}E[\underline{X}^{N}_{s}(\mbox{\bf 1})]ds\leq C_{\ref{U45bnd}}(T^{\prime})(\log N)^{-13/2}X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1}).

(10.28)

Turning to
{\mathcal{V}}_{j,4}
, we use
\Psi_{s}\equiv 0
on
(\tilde{I})^{c}
and

\|\Psi\|_{\infty}\leq 1

to obtain

\Big|\hat{H}^{N,j}(\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s},t_{N},\Psi_{s})-\hat{}\underline{H}^{N,j}(\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s},t_{N},\Psi_{s})\Big|\leq\frac{(\log N)^{3}}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in\tilde{I}}|\hat{H}^{N,j}(\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s},x,t_{N})-\hat{}\underline{H}^{N,j}(\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s},x,t_{N})|.

(10.29)

For
x\in{S_{N}}
we may use (
7.16
), the coupling
(
10.15
), and then duality (recall
(
10.16
)) to see that

\displaystyle\Big|\hat{H}^{N,j}(\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s},x,t_{N})-\hat{}\underline{H}^{N,j}(\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s},x,t_{N})\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|E_{\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s}}[d^{N,j}(x,\xi_{t_{N}}^{N,{\texttt{vm}}})-d^{N,j}(x,\underline{\xi}_{t_{N}}^{N,{\texttt{vm}}})]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq 2\|r\|E_{\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s}}\Bigl[\sum_{y\in\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}(\xi^{N,{\texttt{vm}}}_{t_{N}}(x+y)-\underline{\xi}^{N,{\texttt{vm}}}_{t_{N}}(x+y))\Bigr]

\displaystyle=2\|r\|\sum_{y\in\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}\hat{E}[\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s}(B^{N,x+y}_{t_{N}})-\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s}({\underline{B}}^{N,x+y}_{t_{N}})].

(10.30)

Now define

{\mathcal{A}}(\delta)=\{x\in{S_{N}}\cap\tilde{I}:\inf_{y\in(\tilde{I})^{c}}|x-y|\leq\delta\}\ \ \text{and}\ \ {\mathcal{A}}(\delta)^{\prime}=\{x\in{S_{N}}\cap\tilde{I}:\inf_{y\in(\tilde{I})^{c}}|x-y|>\delta\}.

We will decompose the sum in (
10.29
) into sums over

x\in{\mathcal{A}}(t_{N}^{1/3})
and
x\in{\mathcal{A}}(t_{N}^{1/3})^{\prime}
.
By (
10.30
),

\displaystyle\frac{(\log N)^{3}}{N^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in{\mathcal{A}}(t_{N}^{1/3})}|\hat{H}^{N,j}(\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s},x,t_{N})-\hat{}\underline{H}^{N,j}(\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s},x,t_{N})|

\displaystyle\leq 2\|r\|\frac{(\log N)^{3}}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{\mathcal{A}}(t_{N}^{1/3})}\hat{E}\Bigl[\sum_{y\in\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s}(B^{N,x+y}_{t_{N}})\Bigr]

\displaystyle=2\|r\|\frac{(\log N)^{3}}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{\mathcal{A}}(t_{N}^{1/3})}\sum_{w\in{S_{N}}}\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s}(w)\sum_{y\in\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}\hat{P}(B^{N,y}_{t_{N}}=w-x)

\displaystyle\leq 2\|r\|\frac{(\log N)^{3}}{N^{\prime}}\Big[\sum_{w\notin{\mathcal{A}}(2t_{N}^{1/3})}\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s}(w)\sum_{y\in\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}\hat{P}(|B^{N,y}_{t_{N}}|_{\infty}>t_{N}^{1/3})+|\bar{\mathcal{N}}|\sum_{w\in{\mathcal{A}}(2t_{N}^{1/3})}\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s}(w)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\ref{H-uH1}}|\bar{\mathcal{N}}|(\log N)^{3}\Big[X^{N}_{s}(\mbox{\bf 1})(N^{-1}+t_{N})t_{N}^{-2/3}+X^{N}_{s}({\mathcal{A}}(2t_{N}^{1/3}))\Big],

(10.31)

for some constant
C_{\ref{H-uH1}}
. In the last line we
used
\underline{X}^{N}\leq X^{N}
and Chebychev’s inequality, and in the
next to last inequality we noted that for

w\notin{\mathcal{A}}(2t_{N}^{1/3})
and
x\in{\mathcal{A}}(t_{N})^{1/3}
, we must
have
|w-x|>t_{N}^{1/3}
. To bound

E[X^{N}_{s}({\mathcal{A}}(2t_{N}^{1/3}))]
we will need a bound on the
mean measure similar to Lemma
7.19
, but now in
terms of the
L^{1}
norm of
\Psi:{S_{N}}\to R_{+}
,

\|\Psi\|_{1}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}\Psi(x)
. The result
we need is

E(X^{N}_{s}(\Psi))\leq X_{0}^{N}(P^{N}_{s}(\Psi))+C_{T}X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})(\log N)^{3}\|\Psi\|_{1}\Bigl(1+\log\Bigl(1\vee\frac{1}{\|\Psi\|_{1}}\Bigr)\Bigr)\quad\forall s\leq T^{\prime}.

This follows easily from (
7.103
) (with

c=0
) and (
7.2
) as in the proof of Lemma 8.6 of

[
9
]
. Use this with

\Psi=1\bigl\{{\mathcal{A}}(2t_{N}^{1/3})\bigr\}
to take means in
(
10.31
), recalling that
X^{N}_{0}
is supported on
I
to
bound
X^{N}_{0}(P^{N}_{s}(\Psi))
, and obtain

\frac{(\log N)^{3}}{N}\int_{0}^{(t-t_{N})^{+}}\sum_{x\in{\mathcal{A}}(t_{N}^{1/3})}E\Big[|\hat{H}^{N,j}(\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s},x,t_{N})-\hat{}\underline{H}^{N,j}(\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s},x,t_{N})|\Big]ds\leq C_{\ref{H-uH2}}(T^{\prime})X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})(\log N)^{-1/6}.

(10.32)

See the derivation of (141) in
[
9
]
for the details.

If
x\in{\mathcal{A}}(t_{N}^{1/3})^{\prime}
and
y\in\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}
, then

\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s}(B_{t_{N}}^{N,x+y})-\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s}(\underline{B}_{t_{N}}^{N,x+y})\neq 0
implies that
B^{N,x+y}
exits
\tilde{I}
before
t_{N}
,
and hence moves a distance exceeding
t_{N}^{1/3}
from
x
by
time
t_{N}
. Therefore (
10.30
) implies

\displaystyle\Big|\hat{H}^{N,j}(\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s},x,t_{N},)-\hat{}\underline{H}^{N,j}(\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s},x,t_{N})\Big|\leq 2\|r\|\sum_{y\in\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}\widehat{E}\Big[\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s}(B^{N,x+y}_{t_{N}})1\Big\{\sup_{u\leq t_{N}}|B^{N,x+y}_{u}-x|>t_{N}^{1/3}\Big\}\Big].

With this bound, using Proposition
7.6
(a), we have

\displaystyle\frac{(\log N)^{3}}{N^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t-t_{N}^{+}}\sum_{x\in{\mathcal{A}}(t_{N}^{1/3})^{\prime}}E\Big[\big|\hat{H}^{N,j}(\xi^{N}_{s},x,t_{N})-\hat{}\underline{H}^{N,j}(\xi^{N}_{s},x,t_{N})\big|\Big]ds

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\|r\|(\log N)^{3}}{N^{\prime}}\int_{0}^{t}E\Big[\sum_{w\in{S_{N}}}\sum_{x\in{\mathcal{A}}(t_{N}^{1/3})^{\prime}}\sum_{y\in\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s}(w)\widehat{P}\Big(B^{N,y}_{t_{N}}=w-x,\sup_{u\leq t_{N}}|B^{N,y}_{u}|>t_{N}^{1/3}\Big)\Big]ds

\displaystyle\leq 2\|r\|(\log N)^{3}\sum_{y\in\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}\widehat{P}(\sup_{u\leq t_{N}}|B^{N,y}_{u}|>t_{N}^{1/3})\int_{0}^{t}E[\underline{X}^{N}_{s}(\mbox{\bf 1})]ds

\displaystyle\leq 2\|r\|C_{\ref{p:mobnds}}tX^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})(\log N)^{3}\sum_{y\in\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}\widehat{P}(\sup_{u\leq t_{N}}|B^{N,y}_{u}|^{2}>t_{N}^{2/3})

\displaystyle\leq C_{\ref{H-uH3}}(T^{\prime})X^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})(\log N)^{-10/3},

(10.33)

where the weak
L^{1}
inequality for nonnegative submartingales is used in the last.
Use the above and (
10.32
) in (
10.29
) to
obtain

|{\mathcal{V}}_{j,4}|\leq C_{\ref{U44bnd}}(T^{\prime})(\log N)^{-1/6}X^{N}_{0}(1).

(10.34)

The inequalities (
10.25
), (
10.26
),
(
10.27
), (
10.28
) and (
10.34
) imply
(
10.11
), and we are done.
∎

Proof of Lemma
10.2
.
From
Section
7.1
we may recall the bounding biased
voter model
\bar{\xi}^{N}
with rate function
{\bar{c}}^{N,b}
as in (
7.6
), constructed as in
(
6.2
). We then construct the killed version of

\bar{\xi}
, denoted
\bar{\underline{\xi}}
, using the same equation
with rate function

{\bar{\underline{c}}}^{N,b}(x,\xi)={\bar{c}}^{N,b}(x,\xi)1(x\in\tilde{I}).

We assume all these
processes have the same initial state
\underline{\xi}^{N}_{0}=\xi^{N}_{0}
,
supported on
\tilde{I}
, as our previous processes. In
Section
7.1
we verified condition
(
6.3
) for the rates of
(\xi^{N},\bar{\xi}^{N})
and for
the rates of
(\xi^{N,{\texttt{vm}}},\bar{\xi}^{N})
. The same condition is
then immediate for the corresponding killed processes as one
simply multiplies the required inequalities by
1(x\in\tilde{I})
. So we may now apply (
6.4
) for the killed
processes to conclude that

\underline{\xi}^{N}\leq{\bar{\underline{\xi}}}^{N}\quad\text{and}\quad\underline{\xi}^{N,{\texttt{vm}}}\leq{\bar{\underline{\xi}}}^{N}.

(10.35)

If

\bar{\underline{X}}_{t}=\frac{1}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}{\bar{\underline{\xi}}}^{N}_{t}(x)\delta_{x}
and
c_{0}=\|r\|(2+\underline{p}^{-1})
,
then we may use the last display on p. 113 of
[
11
]
to
conclude that

E[\bar{\underline{X}}_{t}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})]={\underline{X}}^{N}_{0}({\underline{P}}^{N}_{t}(\mbox{\bf 1}))+c_{0}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{(\log N)^{3}}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}{\underline{P}}^{N}_{t-s}(\mbox{\bf 1})(x)(1-{\bar{}\underline{\xi}}^{N}_{s}(x)\sum_{y\in{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}{\bar{}\underline{\xi}}^{N}_{s}(x+y)\,ds.

(10.36)

To see this, first note that the setting in
[
11
]
is
for a general class of voter model perturbations. This
includes our biased voter model with (the notation is from

[
11
]
)
\delta_{N}\equiv 0
, and

\beta_{N}(\{y\})=c_{0}(\log N)^{3}
for
y\in{\mathcal{N}}_{N}
, while

\beta_{N}
is zero otherwise. The above formula then follows
from p. 113 of
[
11
]
. Now take the difference of
(
10.36
) with (
10.4
) and use (
7.39
)
to conclude (recall (
10.35
))

\displaystyle E[{\bar{\underline{X}}}_{t_{N}}(\mbox{\bf 1})-{\underline{X}}^{N}_{t_{N}}(\mbox{\bf 1})]

\displaystyle=E\Bigl[\int_{0}^{t_{N}}\Bigl(c_{0}\Bigl\{\frac{(\log N)^{3}}{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in{S_{N}}}{\underline{P}}^{N}_{t_{N}-s}(\mbox{\bf 1})(x)(1-{\bar{}\underline{\xi}}^{N}_{s}(x))\sum_{y\in{\mathcal{N}}_{N}}{\bar{}\underline{\xi}}^{N}_{s}(x+y)\Bigr\}

\displaystyle\phantom{=E\Bigl[\int_{0}^{t_{N}}\Bigl\{}\ -\sum_{j=2}^{3}d^{N,j}(x,\underline{\xi}^{N}_{s},\mbox{\bf 1})\Bigr)\,ds\Bigr]

(10.37)

\displaystyle\leq c_{0}\int_{0}^{t_{N}}|\bar{\mathcal{N}}|(\log N)^{3}E[{\bar{X}}^{N}_{s}(\mbox{\bf 1})+X^{N}_{s}(\mbox{\bf 1})]\,ds

\displaystyle\leq c_{0}|\bar{\mathcal{N}}|(\log N)^{3}\int_{0}^{t_{N}}[e^{c_{0}(\log N)^{3}s}X_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1})+CX^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1})]\,ds

\displaystyle\leq C(\log N)^{-16}X_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1}),

(10.38)

where Lemma 4.1 of
[
10
]
), and
Proposition
7.6
(a) are used in the next to last
line. The same reasoning (in fact it is simpler as there are
no drift terms) gives

E[{\bar{\underline{X}}}_{t_{N}}(\mbox{\bf 1})-{\underline{X}}^{N,{\texttt{vm}}}_{t_{N}}(\mbox{\bf 1})]\leq C(\log N)^{-16}X_{0}^{N}(\mbox{\bf 1}).

(10.39)

Now argue exactly as in the proof of Lemma
7.2
,
using (
10.38
) and (
10.39
) in place of
Lemma
7.1
, to obtain the following killed
version of Lemma
7.2
(recall now
p=19
from the
definition of
t_{N}
):

There is a

C_{\ref{lemma6.2old}}
so that for
j=2,3
, all
T^{\prime}>0
,

s\in[0,T^{\prime}]
and all
\Phi\in C_{b}([0,T^{\prime}]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2})
,

\displaystyle\qquad E_{\underline{\xi}^{N}_{0}}[|d^{N,j}(s,\underline{\xi}^{N}_{t_{N}},\Phi)-d^{N,j}(s,\underline{\xi}^{N,{\texttt{vm}}}_{t_{N}},\Phi)|]\leq C_{\ref{lemma6.2old}}\|\Phi\|_{\infty}(\log N)^{-13}\underline{X}^{N}_{0}(\mbox{\bf 1}).

(10.40)

The proof of Lemma
10.2
is now completed
using the Markov property just as in the derivation of
Lemma
7.3
. ∎

Proof of Corollary
1.17
. By
Theorem
4.9
and Theorem
1.15
we
have the percolation condition (
4
). The required
result now follows by a comparison to supercritical oriented
percolation and Theorem
1.15
itself, as in
the proof of survival in Proposition 5.3 of
[
11
]
. In
fact, the derivation is now a bit easier as the uniform
bounds proved there are not required. ∎

11
Appendix: The
|{\mathcal{N}}|=8
case of
Lemma
3.3

Recall that for
|{\mathcal{N}}|=8
,

\displaystyle M(k,j)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\text{odd }i\leq j\wedge k}\binom{j}{i}\binom{|8-j}{k-i},\qquad 1\leq k,j\leq 8,

(11.1)

\displaystyle a_{\ell}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\ell}{8}\right)^{q},\qquad 1\leq\ell\leq 8.

(11.2)

Our goal is to verify that if
\bm{\alpha}=\mathbf{a}\mathbf{M}^{-1}
then
there exists a
q_{0}<1
such that

\alpha_{\ell}(q)>0\ \forall\ q_{0}<q<1,\ 1\leq\ell\leq 8.

(11.3)

The conclusion of Lemma
3.3
then follows as
described in Section
3.1
.

It is straightforward to check that
\mathbf{M}
given by
(
11.1
) is

\mathbf{M}=\begin{bmatrix}1&2&3&4&5&6&7&8\\
7&12&15&16&15&12&7&0\\
21&30&31&28&25&26&35&56\\
35&40&35&32&35&40&35&0\\
35&30&25&28&31&26&21&56\\
21&12&13&16&13&12&21&0\\
7&2&5&4&3&6&1&8\\
1&0&1&0&1&0&1&0.\end{bmatrix}

Using
maple
, we obtain

\mathbf{M}^{-1}=\begin{bmatrix}-\frac{3}{64}&-\frac{1}{32}&-\frac{1}{64}&0&\frac{1}{64}&\frac{1}{32}&\frac{3}{64}&\frac{1}{16}\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr-\frac{7}{64}&-\frac{1}{32}&\frac{1}{64}&\frac{1}{32}&\frac{1}{64}&-\frac{1}{32}&-\frac{7}{64}&-\frac{7}{32}\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr-\frac{7}{64}&\frac{1}{32}&\frac{3}{64}&0&-\frac{3}{64}&-\frac{1}{32}&\frac{7}{64}&\frac{7}{16}\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr 0&\frac{5}{64}&0&-\frac{3}{64}&0&\frac{5}{64}&0&-\frac{35}{64}\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\frac{7}{64}&\frac{1}{32}&-\frac{3}{64}&0&\frac{3}{64}&-\frac{1}{32}&-\frac{7}{64}&\frac{7}{16}\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\frac{7}{64}&-\frac{1}{32}&-\frac{1}{64}&\frac{1}{32}&-\frac{1}{64}&-\frac{1}{32}&\frac{7}{64}&-\frac{7}{32}\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\frac{3}{64}&-\frac{1}{32}&\frac{1}{64}&0&-\frac{1}{64}&\frac{1}{32}&-\frac{3}{64}&\frac{1}{16}\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\cr\frac{1}{128}&-\frac{1}{128}&\frac{1}{128}&-\frac{1}{128}&\frac{1}{128}&-\frac{1}{128}&\frac{1}{128}&-\frac{1}{128}\end{bmatrix},

and it is straightforward but tedious to check that this is
indeed correct by verifying that the product of the above matrices is the
identity matrix.

Given
\mathbf{M}^{-1}
and
\mathbf{a}
as above,

\bm{\alpha}=\mathbf{a}\mathbf{M}^{-1}
is given by

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}(q)

\displaystyle=-\frac{3\left(\frac{1}{8}\right)^{q}}{64}-\frac{7\left(\frac{1}{4}\right)^{q}}{64}-\frac{7\left(\frac{3}{8}\right)^{q}}{64}+\frac{7\left(\frac{5}{8}\right)^{q}}{64}+\frac{7\left(\frac{3}{4}\right)^{q}}{64}+\frac{3\left(\frac{7}{8}\right)^{q}}{64}+\frac{1}{128}

\displaystyle\alpha_{2}(q)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\left(\frac{1}{8}\right)^{q}}{32}-\frac{\left(\frac{1}{4}\right)^{q}}{32}+\frac{\left(\frac{3}{8}\right)^{q}}{32}+\frac{5\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{q}}{64}+\frac{\left(\frac{5}{8}\right)^{q}}{32}-\frac{\left(\frac{3}{4}\right)^{q}}{32}-\frac{\left(\frac{7}{8}\right)^{q}}{32}-\frac{1}{128}

\displaystyle\alpha_{3}(q)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\left(\frac{1}{8}\right)^{q}}{64}+\frac{\left(\frac{1}{4}\right)^{q}}{64}+\frac{3\left(\frac{3}{8}\right)^{q}}{64}-\frac{3\left(\frac{5}{8}\right)^{q}}{64}-\frac{\left(\frac{3}{4}\right)^{q}}{64}+\frac{\left(\frac{7}{8}\right)^{q}}{64}+\frac{1}{128}

\displaystyle\alpha_{4}(q)

\displaystyle=\frac{\left(\frac{1}{4}\right)^{q}}{32}-\frac{3\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{q}}{64}+\frac{\left(\frac{3}{4}\right)^{q}}{32}-\frac{1}{128}

\displaystyle\alpha_{5}(q)

\displaystyle=\frac{\left(\frac{1}{8}\right)^{q}}{64}+\frac{\left(\frac{1}{4}\right)^{q}}{64}-\frac{3\left(\frac{3}{8}\right)^{q}}{64}+\frac{3\left(\frac{5}{8}\right)^{q}}{64}-\frac{\left(\frac{3}{4}\right)^{q}}{64}-\frac{\left(\frac{7}{8}\right)^{q}}{64}+\frac{1}{128}

\displaystyle\alpha_{6}(q)

\displaystyle=\frac{\left(\frac{1}{8}\right)^{q}}{32}-\frac{\left(\frac{1}{4}\right)^{q}}{32}-\frac{\left(\frac{3}{8}\right)^{q}}{32}+\frac{5\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{q}}{64}-\frac{\left(\frac{5}{8}\right)^{q}}{32}-\frac{\left(\frac{3}{4}\right)^{q}}{32}+\frac{\left(\frac{7}{8}\right)^{q}}{32}-\frac{1}{128}

\displaystyle\alpha_{7}(q)

\displaystyle=\frac{3\left(\frac{1}{8}\right)^{q}}{64}-\frac{7\left(\frac{1}{4}\right)^{q}}{64}+\frac{7\left(\frac{3}{8}\right)^{q}}{64}-\frac{7\left(\frac{5}{8}\right)^{q}}{64}+\frac{7\left(\frac{3}{4}\right)^{q}}{64}-\frac{3\left(\frac{7}{8}\right)^{q}}{64}+\frac{1}{128}

\displaystyle\alpha_{8}(q)

\displaystyle=\frac{\left(\frac{1}{8}\right)^{q}}{16}-\frac{7\left(\frac{1}{4}\right)^{q}}{32}+\frac{7\left(\frac{3}{8}\right)^{q}}{16}-\frac{35\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{q}}{64}+\frac{7\left(\frac{5}{8}\right)^{q}}{16}-\frac{7\left(\frac{3}{4}\right)^{q}}{32}+\frac{\left(\frac{7}{8}\right)^{q}}{16}-\frac{1}{128}

By pairing off terms it is easy to see that
\alpha_{1}(q)\geq 1/128>0
for all
0\leq q\leq 1
. As described in
Section
3.1
, to prove (
11.3
) it remains
only to verify (
3.12
), so we consider

\alpha^{\prime}_{\ell}(q),2\leq\ell\leq 8
. By differentiating,
plugging in
q=1
and simplifying, we obtain

\displaystyle\alpha_{2}^{\prime}(1)

\displaystyle=\frac{3}{128}\log 2-\frac{3}{256}\log 3+\frac{5}{256}\log 5-\frac{7}{256}\log 7

\displaystyle\alpha_{3}^{\prime}(1)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{64}\log 2+\frac{3}{512}\log 3-\frac{15}{512}\log 5+\frac{7}{512}\log 7

\displaystyle\alpha_{4}^{\prime}(1)

\displaystyle=-\frac{5}{128}\log 2+\frac{3}{128}\log 3

\displaystyle\alpha_{5}^{\prime}(1)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{64}\log 2-\frac{15}{512}\log 3+\frac{15}{512}\log 5-\frac{7}{512}\log 7

\displaystyle\alpha_{6}^{\prime}(1)

\displaystyle=\frac{3}{128}\log 2-\frac{9}{256}\log 3-\frac{5}{256}\log 5+\frac{7}{256}\log 7

\displaystyle\alpha_{7}^{\prime}(1)

\displaystyle=\frac{5}{64}\log 2+\frac{63}{512}\log 3-\frac{35}{512}\log 5-\frac{21}{512}\log 7

\displaystyle\alpha_{8}^{\prime}(1)

\displaystyle=-\frac{101}{128}\log 2+\frac{35}{128}\log 5+\frac{7}{128}\log 7

We need only verify that each of the
above are strictly negative.
Resorting again to
maple
, the above derivatives can
be written in the form

\displaystyle\alpha_{2}^{\prime}(1)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{256}\log\left(\frac{22235661}{200000}\right)

\displaystyle\alpha_{3}^{\prime}(1)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{512}\log\left(\frac{30517578125}{5692329216}\right)

\displaystyle\alpha_{4}^{\prime}(1)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{128}\log\left(\frac{32}{27}\right)

\displaystyle\alpha_{5}^{\prime}(1)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{512}\log\left(\frac{11816941917501}{7812500000000}\right)

\displaystyle\alpha_{6}^{\prime}(1)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{256}\log\left(\frac{61509375}{52706752}\right)

\displaystyle\alpha_{7}^{\prime}(1)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{512}\log\left(\frac{1625582413058972472208552062511444091796875}{1258458428839311554156984626190103821156352}\right)

\displaystyle\alpha_{8}^{\prime}(1)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{128}\log\left(\frac{2535301200456458802993406410752}{2396825584582984447479248046875}\right).

This completes the proof of (
3.12
) for the case

|{\mathcal{N}}|=8
, and so we are done.
